mr president, i am going to discuss something that we have all observed over the last few months, which is the persistent, intensive defiance by erdoğan and turkey, its refusal to implement its obligations with regard to basic and fundamental human rights and the rule of law, the continued occupation of cyprus and, of course, the ongoing attack against its own people. i am referring to the academics, to the kurds in the kurdish provinces of turkey, and so on. my question is very simple, and refers to the commission itself: do you think that our policy towards erdoğan over the last few months has been effective? is it based on the principles of the european union and morals, or on something else?
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster is a good one, but it is not enough to say that the directive is a good one. instead of the primary objective being the well-being and enhancement of people's quality of life, the potential of technology will unfortunately be exploited mainly for the increase of corporate profits and competitiveness. the reforms to complete the digital single market will have a particularly negative impact on smes, labour rights and consumers themselves. the objectives of the digital agenda therefore need to be redefined to put society's interests at the centre. our position is clear: the implementation of the digital market should contribute to the creation of new quality jobs and to the strengthening of labour and consumer rights. it is equally important to ensure both free and open access to the internet and the protection of personal data. the commission will continue to support the development of the european union's policy on the environment, and will continue to support the development of the european union's policy on the environment. the amendments tabled by the european united left are moving in this direction.
in writing. we support the digital single market because we believe that the eu must finally be able to face the digital competition of the usa and asia and create growth potential with thousands of jobs and billions of investments. it is a fact that many young people with great talent in this field are leaving europe because in the eu we have 28 different regulations. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on digital services, in particular by ensuring that the union's digital services are not used for commercial purposes. i believe that in this context it is time to focus on the particularly important issue of the internet. small and isolated countries such as cyprus and other island regions pay high prices for low quality and low speed internet services. the european parliament is of the opinion that access to the internet is a human right and that this right should be guaranteed for all european citizens. but while we support this position, what have we done to that end? i repeat my proposal and ask that island regions such as cyprus be made free and open internet access zones.
in writing. ‒ we reject the report on the request for waiver of immunity of mr czesław adam siekierski.
in writing. ‒ the appropriate position is to vote in favour because the issues for which the waiver of immunity is requested are not related to parliamentary activity and because the mep himself agrees with the waiver of his immunity.
in writing. ‒ we support the report on the request for waiver of immunity of mr czesław adam siekierski.
in writing. ‒ the appropriate position is to vote in favour because the issues for which the waiver of immunity is requested are not related to parliamentary activity and because the mep himself agrees with the waiver of his immunity.
in writing. ‒ the the european union is a contracting party to the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas (iccat) and the regional fisheries management organisation (rfmos) responsible for the management of tuna and tuna-like species in the atlantic ocean and adjacent seas. iccat has the competence to adopt decisions ('recommendations') for the conservation and management of fishery resources in its area of competence, acts which are binding as regards their transposition into union law. we therefore support the report which stresses that uniform application must aim at ensuring a level playing field for all operators involved in tuna fishing, including in particular non-eu operators, otherwise all the efforts and restrictions imposed on eu fishermen will be ineffective if different rules apply to foreign fleets. eu bodies should not be harmed by imposing stricter measures than agreed because the commission was unable to impose similar measures in the iccat negotiations.
in writing. ‒ the proposal aims to transpose into union law the measures of the multiannual bluefin tuna recovery plan in the eastern atlantic and mediterranean adopted by the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas at its annual meeting between 2012 and 2014. the proposal includes technical measures and measures for the transport and caging of live tuna catches. we believe that the appropriate attitude is to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ the proposal aims to transpose the measures of the multiannual bluefin tuna recovery plan for the eastern atlantic and mediterranean into the european acquis, which was adopted by the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas (iccat) at its annual meeting between 2012 and 2014. we voted against the proposal because it includes technical measures such as those concerning the use of stereoscopic cameras to calculate tuna quantities and measures for the transport of live tuna catches, measures which favour the development of the blue economy and, above all, because it supports small-scale fishermen and mediterranean businesses which depend on tuna fishing.
in writing. ‒ the articles 101 to 109 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union (tfeu) contain binding rules on competition in the common internal market, which is based on an open economy with free competition. we support the report and stress that competition policy can contribute to the objectives of the employment and growth agenda, including in areas such as the digital internal market, energy policy, financial services, industrial policy and the fight against tax evasion. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this is contrary to the basic principle of the free exercise of economic policy.
in writing. ‒ the the report promotes the common tax base and continues to seek to deregulate the labour market within the framework of eu competition policies. the report includes some positive amendments on social dumping and the pharmaceutical industry, but despite these we believe that the most appropriate attitude is to vote against.
in writing. ‒ the in its 2014 annual report on eu competition policy, the commission states that more and closer cooperation with the european parliament is needed to ensure that the lisbon treaty is also properly applied in the field of competition. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is well known that the eu is based on an open economy and free competition. abusive agreements by large companies should therefore be prohibited in order to avoid the accumulation of power and the circumvention of legislative provisions which create unfair competition and adversely affect both the national and the community internal market. i think that the paragraph on unfair and illegal clauses and practices used by the banking sector in consumer contracts is also an important reference for cyprus. the commission is invited to examine the matter in detail. however, i disagree with references to a common consolidated corporate tax base which inevitably aim at converging the corporate tax rate of the member states and undermine competitive advantage, particularly in small island and remote member states. i therefore voted against the amendments.
in writing. ‒ the the annual competition report takes stock of the economic situation in the eu each year and focuses on how to make it more competitive. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be consulted on it. the neoliberal forces conceive competitiveness as price reduction through downward pressure on wages and reduction of social security taxes, and by extension social insurance and social benefits. although the text of the report incorporates some of the gue/ngl amendments on social dumping and the pharmaceutical industry, we chose to vote against the text because it promotes the common tax base (something we disagree with as cypriots) and because it continues to promote the deregulation of the labour market due to eu competition policies.
in writing. ‒ we support the report based on the joint agreement of the ministers of education, according to which concerted efforts are needed to prevent and address marginalisation, intolerance, racism and radicalisation, and to maintain a framework of equal opportunities for all. the report identifies opportunities as well as challenges and makes concrete recommendations for action at all levels of governance as well as at eu level, including through the increased use of culture in the eu's external actions and development agenda, as it recognises the unique role that culture can play in peace processes, conflict resolution and crisis prevention.
in writing. ‒ the in the light of the terrorist attacks in france and denmark in early 2015, the report aims to tackle intolerance, racism, radicalisation and marginalisation by promoting intercultural dialogue in the field of education in eu values. we supported the report because it takes into account the great importance of social justice and equality in building a common approach to eu intercultural dialogue, because it seeks to enhance cultural diversity in all youth programmes as well as in the fields of culture and media, because it promotes access to multicultural and multilingual skills for teachers and, finally, because it encourages the eu institutions to broaden their analysis to all forms of radicalisation and political violence in order to tackle all forms of extremism and discrimination.
in writing. ‒ the today we live in a multicultural society, in a time of coexistence under the same roof of different cultures and mindsets. the eu itself is a prime example. despite their different origins, eu citizens are described as european citizens. this approach is an important weapon in the fight against discrimination, racism and intolerance, and it also fosters teamwork and tolerance of 'different'. the eu's founding values, such as democracy, equality and respect for human rights, which are enshrined in the eu charter of fundamental rights, underline the need for intercultural dialogue and acceptance of cultural diversity and pluralism. this can be achieved through the education and training of young people. education systems should promote healthy and democratic dialogue and public participation with a view to developing culture as an important peaceful means of addressing humanitarian crises. they must also promote the strengthening of european identity while preserving national and other distinctive features. i therefore support this resolution and would like to stress that the eu itself must contribute to this effort through specific development and other programmes.
in writing. ‒ we voted in favour of the report because it encourages the exchange of good practice to promote intercultural dialogue and cooperation between schools and universities in different member states. at the same time, inter-religious dialogue is promoted, which promotes understanding, mutual respect and peace, especially at a time of global challenges such as the refugee issue or the rise of nationalism in european societies, challenges that are of course a byproduct of the capitalist crisis and economic and social inequalities.
in writing. ‒ we support the own-initiative report because it is a first step in taking stock of the legislative work in the financial services sector in the eu and because it helps to address the shortcomings that have been observed in the legislation on financial services. the report also takes into account concerns that the impact of individual legislative measures and their interaction has not been adequately analysed and that the cumulative impact of the legislation as a whole has not been fully assessed so far. a thorough impact assessment of the financial services framework is necessary and should be repeated regularly.
in writing. ‒ the the report clearly advocates a capital markets union, arguing that the eu has an obligation to help businesses (especially small and medium-sized enterprises) to have easy access to stock exchanges throughout the union. as regards the future, it promotes direct controls from brussels over the authorities of the member states, something we have always disagreed with because it actually completely destroys the sovereignty of the member states and concentrates all powers in brussels. in view of our disagreement with both the cmu and the accumulation of political decision-making and control privileges in brussels, we consider the vote to be the appropriate one.
in writing. ‒ the the report clearly advocates capital markets union because it considers that the eu has an obligation to help businesses (especially small and medium-sized enterprises) to have easy access to stock exchanges throughout the union. this will of course lead with mathematical precision to even greater capital accumulation by large european groups, but also to the strengthening of monopoly capital at european level with a view to its 'competitiveness' at world level. the report merely praises the steps taken so far towards completing the capital markets union and calls for the framework to be brought into line with global data, while at the same time promoting a corresponding deregulation of companies' access to the stock exchange so that they can enter stock exchanges in other countries. it also advocates direct controls from brussels on the authorities of the member states in the future, something we have always disagreed with because it actually completely dissolves the sovereignty of the member states and concentrates all powers in brussels. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done.
in writing. ‒ differences between men and women remain visible in careers in the business world. we support the report because women's entrepreneurship deserves special attention, since equal economic independence is not yet a given within the european union.
in writing. ‒ the the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. it is true that there are many obstacles for women entrepreneurs, but our main priority, from a left feminist point of view and with the aim of gender equality, is different. the report makes no mention of the important role of the public sector or the negative effects of recent austerity policies. we are afraid that this report may be used to justify measures to promote female entrepreneurship as the only solution to the problem of improving the living conditions of women and we are also afraid that it will simply create more space for the private financial sector. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect.
in writing. ‒ the one of the eu's objectives is to develop the entrepreneurship of european citizens, as the figures show that europe has a lower rate compared to countries such as the usa. for example, as the explanatory report to the resolution states, the entrepreneurship rate in europe was 12% in 2009 and 27% in the us. it is expected that the promotion of entrepreneurship will be able to help the economic growth and prosperity of the member states by creating new jobs and by increasing initiatives towards the exploitation of the skills of european citizens. the vote on this resolution is expected to give particular prominence to the development of women's entrepreneurship, which is important considering that only 31% of the self-employed population are women. it is imperative to combat the barriers to female entrepreneurship with the ultimate aim of equal economic independence for both sexes. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on gender equality and the implementation of the gender equality strategy, in particular through the implementation of the gender equality strategy.
in writing. ‒ the we abstained on the report on women's entrepreneurship in europe because we considered it to be incomplete and contained some problematic references. as a group, we are certainly in favour of the empowerment of women and gender equality in all areas of socioeconomic life. but for us, equality between men and women is not achieved either through policies to support private capital or by promoting female entrepreneurship. gender equality can only be achieved by effectively combating stereotypes, violence and inequalities in workplaces and employment opportunities, as the majority of people filling part-time and flexible jobs are women. the creation of conditions for equal participation in decision-making centres and the creation of modern social structures with high-quality social services are also necessary. the report also makes no mention of the harmful effects of austerity policies from the point of view of equal treatment between men and women.
in writing. ‒ we support the report because we believe that youth unemployment, at such alarming levels, is one of the most intractable problems in the eu today, with some five million unemployed and, at the same time, two million job vacancies. this situation poses a serious threat to the social cohesion and economic development of the eu. the report stresses that a key priority of the commission is to 'give a new impetus to employment, growth and investment' and recalls that in its 2015 work programme, the commission committed to take initiatives to promote integration and employability in the labour market, through measures to support member states in their efforts to reintegrate young people into working life. we must also reiterate that parliament has, at regular intervals, proposed different solutions, because it believes that employment, education and training of young people must be one of the main political priorities for the eu.
in writing. ‒ the report deals with a very important issue, namely youth unemployment. unfortunately, it contains elements such as the interconnection of the business world with education systems, the mobility of young people for internships and jobs, or even voluntary unpaid work, which do not allow us to vote in favour of it.
in writing. ‒ the young people today are a vulnerable group of citizens who have been greatly affected by the economic crisis affecting the eu in recent years. the figures speak for themselves when we consider that 4.5 million young people aged 15 to 24 are currently unemployed throughout europe. the problem is particularly acute in greece and cyprus. unemployment rates are particularly high, with the result that an increasing number of young people are moving abroad in search of work. both the root cause of unemployment and an important means of combating it can be found in higher education. there is a lack of skills needed in the jobs available, because young people choose to study well-established occupations that do not meet the needs of society today. the need to equip young people with the appropriate skills required by today's labour market, while taking into account future needs, is therefore urgent. the development of skills such as adaptability and communication, which are essential in any profession, should be a primary concern of education systems. i therefore wish to vote in favour of this resolution in order to be part of the effort to eliminate youth unemployment in the eu.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report on combating unemployment, which in recent years has been a major challenge for the european union, a challenge linked to its social cohesion and economic development. it is worth noting that this resolution, which is accompanied by an opinion of the committee on culture and education of the european parliament, highlights the problem of young people's inability to enter the labour market because of the weakness of the member states' training programmes. i can only welcome the now declared political direction of the eu towards a comprehensive training of young people with continuous and lifelong learning programmes to replace a sterile education policy which ignores the needs of the labour market and cannot adapt the education systems of the member states to reality.
in writing. ‒ the the report also calls for the development of a european strategy for youth unemployment, which would include the development of a european strategy for youth employment, and the development of a european strategy for youth employment. there is obviously no reference to a public investment policy for industrialization or to a long-term state job creation policy which will train young people to get jobs there and guarantee the creation of parallel industries to absorb the young unemployed. on the contrary, it leaves the problem of youth unemployment entirely in the hands of the free market, even going so far as to accept as legitimate unpaid internships as a means of gaining experience to strengthen the cvs of young people. although the gue/ngl tabled amendments to improve the report, elements such as the complete dependence of the fight against unemployment on the already failed youth guarantee programme and the utopian juncker plan for investment do not allow us to vote in favour of the report because we see nothing promising in it beyond the trite, failed policies already in place.
in writing. ‒ the i vote in favour of the report in which the european parliament, in agreement with the commission, points out that the governance and timely completion of the digital single market is a shared responsibility of the european parliament, the council and the commission, while urging the commission to work with the social partners and to seek their full participation in the decision-making process. i believe that the increased use of digital technologies can improve citizens' access to information and culture, increase job opportunities and provide them with more product choices. it also has the potential to modernise and improve governance and administration in the eu and its member states. promoting a vibrant economy is essential to boost innovation and to enable innovative enterprises to develop large-scale activities. the digital single market strategy is structured around three pillars: (1) access: better access for consumers and businesses to digital products and services across europe, (2) environment: creating appropriate conditions and a level playing field for the development of digital networks and innovative services, (3) economy and society: maximising the growth potential of the digital economy.
in writing. ‒ the this is an own-initiative report on the digital single market, which contains positive references such as the need to protect smes and labour rights, the need to safeguard personal data, to strengthen safety and health measures for workers, and to preserve the character of the internet as an open, neutral, secure and inclusive platform in the interest of citizens. at the same time, however, several negative references are identified, such as proposals to further harmonise legislation to the benefit of large companies, the promotion of the digital market as a tool to serve eu foreign policy objectives, and the promotion of european satellite navigation systems. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' in the context of the directive on the free movement of goods. for these reasons we consider abstinence to be appropriate.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report which provides for a strategy for the creation of a free and secure digital single market. according to a commission study, the digital single market could contribute around eur 520 billion to the eu-28's gdp. however, european citizens face obstacles when using online services and tools. only 15% of citizens buy online from another eu country and only 7% of smes sell online from another eu country. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council adopt a common position on the proposal for a directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the labelling, presentation and advertising of foodstuffs for sale to the ultimate consumer. the adoption of the report is aimed at removing the obstacles, always giving priority to consumer protection and the strengthening of the european entrepreneurial spirit. the commission should also be able to provide the necessary information to the member states and the commission on the implementation of the union's policy on the digital single market. i also stress the importance of high-speed internet access for all european citizens without discrimination.
in writing. ‒ the we abstained on this report because it advocates the creation of a european digital single market in which new technologies will be used to further develop competitiveness and to serve market needs. the aim of the above is nothing other than to serve the interests of big business and multinationals. we are opposed to such a policy, since we believe that technological development and the digital market should be used to improve people's quality of life. the report also mentions the need for a common eu-wide consolidated corporate tax base and a single vat system, which is particularly negative for cyprus. it should be noted, however, that the report also makes some positive references to the need to protect smes and labour rights, to safeguard personal data, to strengthen safety and health measures for workers, and to maintain the internet as an open, neutral, secure and inclusive platform in the interest of citizens.

the commission has also adopted a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report because it seeks to strengthen the right to a fair trial in criminal proceedings by establishing minimum rules on certain parameters relating to the presumption of innocence and the right of the accused to be present at his trial. by establishing minimum rules for the protection of the procedural rights of suspects or accused persons, this directive will enhance the confidence of member states in the criminal justice systems of other member states and may therefore contribute to facilitating the mutual recognition of decisions in criminal matters.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the after a very good report by the alde rapporteur, mrs griesbeck, our group voted in favour of the report in the libe debate. the first reading of the report by the council was much more conservative than the report by the european parliament. the two institutions had a long discussion and in the end a compromise was reached somewhere in the middle. however, the final report contains important victories for the european parliament, such as the failure to overturn the presumption of innocence. that was one of the key elements for us. in general, we believe that the directive respects the case law of the european court of human rights and in some cases goes even further. that is why we voted in favour of the report.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the commission has tabled three notable proposals for directives concerning the presumption of innocence and the right of the accused to be present at criminal proceedings. protected by the eu treaties as well as by the charter of fundamental rights and the echr, these two rights are essential means of protecting the suspect or accused in criminal proceedings and guarantee fair trial for the proper administration of justice. in this context, and in order to ensure that the trial is conducted in an impartial manner, some amendments should be made to the commission's proposals. these amendments, which i consider important and which i support, are, for example, the prohibition of premature guilt assessments from the exercise of the right to remain silent, which should not have a negative impact on the outcome of the trial, and the prohibition of making accusations in the media before the accused is sentenced. in addition, it should be clear that the burden of proof of the accused's guilt is beyond reasonable doubt, as is the case in cypriot law, and therefore if at the end of the trial the court retains any doubt as to the accused's guilt it should acquit the accused. finally, the number of cases where the defendant is tried in absentia should be reduced to an absolute minimum.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the we supported the report on the strengthening of certain aspects of the presumption of innocence and the right of the accused to be present at trial in criminal proceedings, as the procedures must first of all respect fundamental rights and be non-discriminatory. the law must be equal to all. otherwise, there is growing doubt and lack of confidence in the justice of society, which leads to violent events and an increase in crime.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the right balance must be struck between the greatest possible protection of consumers and the additional costs this entails for producers, so that small and medium-sized enterprises are not driven into bankruptcy. the shadow rapporteur of our group tabled five amendments in this direction to the commission. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the commission has also adopted a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. ‒ the we reject the report on the proposal for a regulation on gas appliances to replace directive 2009/142/ec. this directive is an example of harmonisation of union legislation to ensure the free movement of gas appliances and has contributed significantly to the completion and functioning of the single market by harmonising the conditions for the placing on the market and/or putting into service of gas appliances falling within its scope in relation to risks to gas safety and the rational use of energy. it is recalled that the gas sector plays an important role in the eu economy.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ it is a technical report covering all the formal provisions of eu legislation on goods and setting out the obligations of the relevant economic operators (producers, agents, importers and distributors). that is why we voted in favour of it.

in writing. ‒ we support the association agreement with moldova, which includes a bilateral safeguard clause. thus, preferences may be temporarily suspended in case their application may lead to an unexpected and significant increase in imports causing economic injury to the domestic industry of the importing party (i.e. it is possible either to suspend further liberalisation of duties or to reinstate the most-favoured-nation (mfn) tariff rate). in addition, the agreement also contains a so-called anti-circumvention mechanism, which provides for the possibility of reinstating the 'most favoured state' tariff rate when imports of certain agricultural products from moldova exceed a certain threshold, without duly justifying their exact origin.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report implements provisions adopted under the eu-moldova association agreement. it concerns substantive safeguard clauses for the imposition of measures when imports of certain products reach a level which is considered to be likely to affect producers in the eu or when there is no proper justification of their exact origin (circumvention mechanism). the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the eu-moldova association agreement to include the eu's neighbours in the region, the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, the former yugoslav republic of macedonia and the former yugoslav republic of macedonia.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the we disagree with the content of the eu association agreement with moldova because it fully serves the interests of the eu's ruling circles. it creates interdependent relations - economic and political - with its partners and promotes extreme competition to the benefit of big business and to the detriment of the interests of society and the people. however, we voted against this report as it specifically refers to the safeguard clause which can be imposed when a product originating in moldova is imported into the union in such increased quantities as to cause economic damage to the domestic industry of the eu. the report also refers to the anti-circumvention mechanism, which provides for the possibility of reinstating the gsp tariff rate when imports of agricultural products from moldova exceed a certain threshold without duly justifying their exact origin. the circumvention clauses are important to prevent imports into the union of non-moldovan products via moldova which would benefit from the agreement and circumvent the rules of origin requirements.
in writing. ‒ the eu-georgia association agreement includes the so-called anti-circumvention mechanism, which provides for the possibility of reinstating the most-favoured-state tariff rate when imports of certain agricultural products from georgia exceed a certain threshold without duly justifying their exact origin. we therefore support the report and consider that a regulation of the european parliament and the council is required to incorporate into eu law the necessary means to enable the anti-circumvention mechanism to be implemented.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report implements provisions adopted under the eu-georgia association agreement. it essentially concerns the introduction of measures where there is no proper justification of their exact origin (circumvention mechanism). despite our disagreement with the content of the eu-georgia association agreement, we voted in favour of the report because the circumvention mechanism is an important tool to prevent non-georgian products from being imported into the union through the country.

in writing. ‒ the we support the motion for a resolution and recall that reducing economic, social and environmental disparities between regions is a major objective of european cohesion policy. the impact of the economic crisis has seriously damaged the development potential of many disadvantaged regions, including islands, and reversed the long-term trend of convergence of gdp and unemployment rates across the eu, increasing poverty and social exclusion and hampering the achievement of the union's long-term objective of economic, social and territorial cohesion. we stress that islands, as peripheral regions on the eu's external borders, are particularly vulnerable to today's challenges (globalisation, demographic challenges, climate change, energy supply and, particularly for the southern regions, exposure to increasing migratory flows). for the above reasons, we call on the commission to draw up an 'eu strategic framework for islands', as we call on member states and regional and local authorities to play an important role in development strategies for islands based on a vertical approach involving all levels of governance, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, in the perspective of ensuring sustainable development for eu islands.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the we supported the report on island-building because it proposes concrete measures to address the permanent handicaps faced by europe's islands. the text also points out the unique difficulties faced by the southern island regions of europe due to increased migration flows and calls for the continuation of the special tax arrangements for islands, 'in particular those countries undergoing economic adjustment programmes'. in particular, it calls on the commission to (a) create, on the basis of article 174 tfeu, which recognises the permanent handicaps of island regions, a homogeneous group consisting of all island regions, (b) take into account, in addition to gdp, other statistical indicators reflecting economic and social vulnerability as a result of permanent natural handicaps, (c) produce an in-depth study/analysis on the extra costs of living in island regions, as regards the transport system, energy supply and access to markets, and finally to submit a communication on the eu agenda for new zealand and a follow-up to the development of access to new zealand.

in writing. ‒ the we voted against the resolution as it aims to further strengthen the role of regional and local authorities in the formulation of national policies on local and regional environmental issues. in the context of cohesion policy, which has been given legal force by regulation 1303/2013 on the operation of the esi funds, it has been set as an objective that the views and positions of local authorities should be taken into account in the consultations on regulations and the approval of operational programmes (programmes financed by the esi funds in each country). however, the degree of involvement of local and regional authorities in these negotiations varies across the member states and further strengthening of this role is necessary to implement the objectives of cohesion policy more effectively.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the yemen has been in a long-running conflict, which intensified in 2015 when saudi arabia, as the head of a us-backed coalition, began bombing yemen, while imposing a near-total import embargo on the country. this situation has led to hunger and poverty for the people, while attacks on civilians, medical personnel and members of humanitarian organisations have caused huge loss of life. the conflict is yet another attempt by the us and its allies to maintain their geopolitical control and dominance in the region. the eu is also complicit in this crime, which, by tolerating the dictatorships in the region and its member states, which continue to export heavy defence equipment to saudi arabia, is contributing to the worsening of the conflict in the country. the serious deterioration of the humanitarian situation in yemen requires immediate humanitarian assistance, and we therefore consider that a vote in favour of the joint resolution is the appropriate course of action.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the hague convention of 15 november 1965 established a system whereby judicial documents may be served in another contracting state. of the 28 member states of the union, 26 are already parties to the convention. the other two, austria and malta, have expressed interest in joining. malta has already submitted the accession documents but has made a declaration that its accession will not take effect until the adoption of this decision. austria awaits this decision in order to complete the accession formalities. the report is a procedural one, and we have voted in favour of it.

in writing. ‒ the despite many improvements, there are some problems related to the correct application of certain vague and legal terms such as 'humanitarian treatment' of animals. another problem is that stray pets are considered 'wild animals'. this concept can serve to reduce the legal protection of stray animals. dogs and cats should always be considered pets, whether or not they are stray, even if they are no longer under the direct control of humans. finally, one of the biggest problems was the very high number of proposed implementing and delegated acts, which made it difficult to foresee how the law could work in practice. that is why we voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ the the new regulation, which deals with the control of communicable diseases such as avian influenza and african swine fever, has a preventive role. this is because of the increased risk of transmission of these and other diseases and the parallel need to reduce the use of antibiotics in animals to reduce the risk of microbial resistance. the regulation will oblige member states to provide regular reports with full data on the frequency of use of these medicines, thus improving knowledge at european level. i am voting against the adoption of this regulation in order to establish the legal framework and rules governing the control and prevention of the transmission of animal diseases in accordance with the findings of modern scientific studies. the regulation should also be consistent with eu legislation on animal welfare and animal welfare. finally, the adoption of this regulation will lay the foundations for more prudent use of medicines and further improvement of livestock farming, with clear responsibility for those involved in this sector in case of non-compliance with the principles of good animal husbandry and responsible use of antibiotics in animals.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and stress that in the current context of a continuous decline in the consumption of fresh fruit and vegetables and dairy products, especially by children, and the increasing incidence of childhood obesity due to consumer habits oriented towards highly processed foods, the provision of district aid to finance the provision of selected agricultural products to children attending educational institutions can contribute to the promotion of healthy eating habits and the consumption of local products.
in writing. ‒ the although the objectives of the programme are commendable, they tend to be neutralized in a series of articles requiring the diversion of funds from the distribution of fruit and vegetables to advertising purposes. for example, article 23 (8) requires schools to disclose their participation in the scheme and the fact that it is eu-funded. although article 23a (8) states that member states may use any appropriate communication tools they wish (websites, posters, campaigns), articles 24 and 25 allow the commission to adopt delegated acts requiring schools to implement these actions. we believe that this creates a deficit procedure for using funds for a system that is already in operation and we therefore abstained.
in writing. ‒ the the resolution is a small part of the wider effort to promote healthy eating habits among children and young people by using local products that are not high in added harmful substances. the fight against childhood obesity, with all its consequences on the psycho-physical composition of children, should continue to be a priority for the union, given that the surveys carried out at national level are worrying in terms of its incidence and rate. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the foodstuffs are not used in the same way as the foodstuffs intended for human consumption. to be effective, this action should be accompanied by supporting educational measures to inform and understand the beneficial effect of proper nutrition on their health as well as continuous evaluation of this overall strategy for more targeted results. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect.
in writing. ‒ the i have supported a proposal for legislation to monitor the eating habits of children. as stated in the draft law, the member states should - as a general principle - promote healthy eating habits, local food, organic farming and the fight against food waste. this regulation strengthens and extends the previous regime for monitoring the eating habits of children in schools by adding further products of compulsory consumption. in particular, the list of products eligible for european funding is amended, giving priority to fresh and local products as opposed to processed foods such as soups, compost, juices, yoghurt and cheese. these foods will only be given in addition to fresh fruit, vegetables and milk or lactose-free milk. i can only support and support in the most positive way possible eu initiatives aimed at monitoring the eating habits of children.

in writing. ‒ the the aim of the proposal is to create a framework to improve the competitiveness of ports and to 'modernise' them in order to create the conditions necessary to attract investment and to achieve the liberalisation of port services. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. the text improves the commission proposal in part by deleting proposals to ensure access to the market for port services, adding important references to the protection of labour rights and collective agreements and including positive references to the need to ensure the health and safety of workers. however, despite the positive reports, we do not think that we could have a positive view of this regulation. that's why we voted against it.
in writing. ‒ the the text improves the commission proposal in part by deleting most of the proposals on securing access to the market for port services, adding important references to the protection of labour rights and collective agreements and including positive references to the need to ensure the health and safety of workers. however, we cannot ignore the fact that many individual reforms are being promoted to reduce costs and increase competitiveness as a step towards the ultimate goal which is still the full liberalisation of port services. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations, and i would like to ask the commissioner to give us a clear indication of the extent to which the commission is prepared to take part in the negotiations. we have voted against the text for these reasons.
in writing. ‒ we support the report, which establishes a single legal framework for the development, compilation and dissemination of data on harmonised indices of consumer prices (hicps) and house prices (hicps) at eu, national and sub-national level. hicps provide an inflation indicator which is used, inter alia, as a valuable source of information for the deflation of economic variables such as wages, rents and interest rates and which therefore forms the basis for economic and monetary policy decisions. it is therefore essential that those statistics are objective, unbiased and comparable across all member states and product sectors.
in writing. ‒ the report focuses on the indicators which determine the economic policies of each member state, as they can lower or raise the value of wages and social rights in the member states. the harmonization of the two means that one of the most important instruments of the budgetary policy of the state is transferred to the commission. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the consumer is not excluded from the market.
in writing. ‒ the the resolution upgrades the existing single european information system on harmonised indices of consumer prices (hicps) and house prices (hicps). these indicators are an important means of information on inflation and related issues, which are of great importance for the economic and monetary policy of the union. this regulation has been in force since 1995 and needs to be updated to take account of new market developments in order to achieve more effective price comparability with the ultimate aim of achieving equal treatment of similar product categories. i am voting against this motion for a resolution because i believe that a new legal framework for the hicp is needed to reflect new developments in this field in order to integrate systems and prevent divergent national practices that undermine the provision of reliable statistical results. the resolution calls on the commission to submit a proposal to amend or replace the current regulation 2494/95 by including the impact of tax changes on inflation and providing preliminary estimates by member states on a monthly basis in order to better analyse them for more accurate results.
in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report on the grounds that it promotes the harmonization of consumer indicators. these are the indicators which determine the economic policies of each state since they can lower or raise the value of wages and social rights in the member states. the harmonization of the two means that one of the most important instruments of the budgetary policy of the state is transferred to the commission.
in writing. ‒ we support the report considering that member states have the primary responsibility for the collection of own resources, also stressing that the rationalisation of public spending and the protection of the eu's financial interests should be central elements of the eu's policy to strengthen citizens' confidence by ensuring the correct, effective and efficient use of their money.
in writing. ‒ the member states and the commission share responsibility for the implementation of about 80% of the union budget and member states are mainly responsible for collecting own resources, including in the form of vat and customs duties. the diversity of the legal and administrative systems of the member states presents a complexity which sometimes makes efforts to combat irregularities and fraud more difficult. the report, although flawed, is in the right context and proposes ways and means of strengthening this effort, which is why we voted in favour of it.
in writing. ‒ the the protection of the confidence of european citizens through a clean and efficient system for the use of their money is a matter of paramount importance. the rational use of public expenditure is a prerequisite, and this can be achieved by regular checks on the performance of national systems, which unfortunately often differ in practice, making it difficult to detect irregularities and fraud. the protection of the eu's financial interests is an immediate objective, as is the fight against fraud, since corruption exists in all member states, hindering the smooth functioning of the system and reducing the eu's competitiveness in the global economy and market. the cultural impact and the attack on the principles of the rule of law, values which are the cornerstone of the eu, are equally notable. the european anti-fraud office (olaf) has an important role to play in strengthening action to combat corruption and fraud. i am ignoring the findings and the changes proposed in the annual report because i consider it to be the duty of the member states to protect the financial interests of the union from criminal offences which have economic and moral consequences. this requires that member states cooperate directly with olaf and grant it further procedural rights to ensure the effective implementation of its recommendations.
in writing. ‒ the report calls for the creation of common indicators of fraud so that it is identified and recognised in the same way in all member states, while promoting minimal protection for whistle blowers. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.
in writing. ‒ the we support the report, stressing that gender mainstreaming has become internationally accepted as a strategy for achieving gender equality. the integration of the gender dimension has two dimensions: the commission's proposal for a directive on the integration of the gender dimension in the content of various policies and the representation of women and men in the various policy areas is also included. both dimensions - gender representation and gender-sensitive content - must be taken into account at all stages of the policy-making process. structural inequalities between the sexes still exist in our society and gender-sensitive policies are therefore a crucial element of a sound gender equality strategy.
in writing. ‒ the the report stresses the need for gender dimension amendments, gender impact assessment and gender budgeting to mainstream the gender dimension in all policy areas. stresses the need for more cooperation between the various committees, which is essential, and calls on the high level group to submit a gender equality roadmap showing how to increase the representation of women in middle and senior management to 40% by 2020. to better integrate the gender dimension into all policy areas, the report proposed further development of gender mainstreaming tools such as gender dimension modifications. it also called on the commission to make gender impact assessments in new legislative or policy proposals to ensure the protection of women's rights. given that equality between men and women is recognised by the eu as a fundamental right, a common value of the eu and a necessary condition for achieving the eu's objectives of growth, employment and social cohesion, we believe that the report has made a positive contribution in this direction and therefore voted in favour of it.
in writing. ‒ the the resolution concerns the integration of the gender dimension in the various policies and the representation of men and women in the policy-making area with a view to promoting gender equality in the european parliament. although gender equality is now an undeniable right and value of the eu, it must be achieved not only by prohibition but also by positive measures to eliminate discrimination on grounds of sex. the parliament's committee on women's rights and gender equality (femm) is responsible for mainstreaming the gender dimension in the ep's political and legislative processes. there is also the network of meps responsible for promoting gender equality in the ep committees. despite the existence of these specific mobilizations, research has shown the lack of an action plan for the practical implementation of gender equality. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one. i am voting in favour of this resolution and welcome the proposals it contains, such as strengthening cooperation with national parliaments, more effective cooperation between femm and the network of members and the establishment of an appropriate framework for the effective integration of the gender dimension in the policies of the ep.
in writing. ‒ the the integration of the gender dimension in the preparation, design, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of policies, regulatory measures and spending programmes, with a view to promoting equality between women and men and combating discrimination, is a positive development. the report proposes measures to strengthen and increase the effectiveness and efficiency of the existing gender mainstreaming structure. however, what is overlooked in the report is that gender equality cannot be understood in the context of the policies promoted by the eu on a daily basis. the report does suggest measures which may improve the parity issue to some extent, but unfortunately these proposals cannot coexist and be improved further in the long term because of the other measures being promoted by the eu. it is therefore pointless to talk about equal treatment for men and women if we do not at the same time fight for the strengthening of the rights of all people. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. that is why we voted in favour.

s&d), in writing. ‒ the the right to a fair trial is a fundamental right of every citizen, enshrined in the constitutions of almost all eu member states and protected by the charter of fundamental rights and the european convention on human rights. the need for safeguarding is even more pressing in criminal cases involving the possibility of depriving a person of liberty, let alone when that person is a minor. procedural safeguards or procedural rules governing the judicial procedure form an integral part of this right. national justice systems should therefore ensure all guarantees of a fair and impartial trial, taking into account the special needs of such minors. the resolution rightly points out that there should be special juvenile courts, staffed by specially trained judges, with the state providing free legal aid directly to the arrested juvenile. special mention should be made of interrogation methods, which may not be the same as those used for adult suspects. finally, the measures imposed on minors should be of a reforming rather than punitive nature, taking into account the young person's psychological makeup. this requires the imposition of a prison sentence as a last resort and detention in special juvenile prisons.

in writing. ‒ we support the report on the licensing, manufacture, marketing, distribution, safety of medicines and the use of veterinary medicinal products.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the over time it has become clear that the needs of the veterinary sector differ significantly from those of the human sector in relation to medicines. for example, the price-setting mechanisms in the veterinary sector follow a completely different logic. therefore, prices for veterinary medicinal products are usually significantly lower than for medicinal products intended for human use. we voted against the report as it proposes measures to address such problems as (a) the lack of availability of veterinary medicinal products, (b) administrative burdens, (c) reduced competitiveness and innovation and (d) the public health risk of antimicrobial resistance (amr).
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the recent commission proposal for a new regulation on veterinary medicinal products completely revises the existing legislation (directives 2001/82/ec and ec 726/2004). the new regulation will regulate the whole process of manufacturing, licensing, marketing, distribution and use of veterinary medicinal products by the parties concerned. the need for a modern regulatory framework reflecting the specific needs of the veterinary sector at european and national level led me to vote in favour of this initiative. the commission's proposal aims to improve access to veterinary medicines, even for smaller markets, which i consider desirable and necessary, and this is something that should be given special consideration and will help to increase the availability of these products in general by reducing the administrative burden of their distribution. it also aims to promote and enhance the competitiveness and innovation of the veterinary medicines market by providing incentives for new ideas and improving the quality of existing medicines. particular attention should be paid to the issue of antibiotics, in particular the setting of conditions for their authorisation and the prohibition of their distribution via the internet. the protection of the environment is, after all, the guiding principle behind all this.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report clearly defines the conditions under which antibiotics may be prescribed and sold. we voted against the report because the market for veterinary medicines must be separated from the market for medicines for human use, but at the same time the health of the animals themselves must be safeguarded by the use of medicines and antibiotics, without the use of genetically modified organisms. it is important that the rules on medicinal products for human use and the rules on veterinary medicinal products agree on this point.
in writing. ‒ we support the report on the establishment of community procedures for the authorisation and supervision of medicinal products for human and veterinary use and the establishment of a european medicines agency.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report's positive aspects are the recognition of subsidiarity and proportionality as basic principles for the functioning of the eu and the need for greater involvement of national parliaments in the european legislative process (while underlining the protection of the union's exclusive competences). in this context, one of the proposals in the report is to strengthen the national parliaments even further in the legislative process. however, there is no criticism of the content of eu initiatives/proposals/legislation, especially as regards the negative economic impact they have/could have on european citizens. that's why we held abstinence.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality are indisputably fundamental principles of the union, enshrined in article 5 of the eu treaty. all european legislation must be consistent with these principles, which strengthen the legal basis and the legality of the union's legislative process. while i welcome the efforts made in recent years to implement the two principles in the union's policies, i would like to stress the need to further strengthen the mechanism for monitoring their implementation through effective cooperation between the european and national legislative bodies. the resolution also provides for dialogue with the member states at the initial stage of any legislative procedure and for the reasoned opinions of national parliaments to be taken into account, which have the first say on matters not falling within the union's exclusive competence. however, there are no further significant means of monitoring the principle of subsidiarity, nor of monitoring the consequences of its non-observance in the legislative negotiations with the states. the principle of subsidiarity, along with the principle of proportionality, whereby the means used are proportionate to the purpose served, are the key guarantees of the legality of the union's legislative process, and that is why i am voting against this resolution.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ although the report contains a number of positive references (protection of workers and better use of existing european structures), a number of amendments tabled by our group to substantially improve the text were rejected. therefore, we abstained.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the benefits of the single market are undeniable and should therefore be protected and strengthened through european legislation. it is therefore a common responsibility of all of us who are part of the legislative process to continually strive for its improvement. all the union's institutions can, through proper and effective cooperation, improve these policies while applying the principle of subsidiarity. this is coupled with appropriate checks that the measures imposed are implemented by the member states. the secondary rules are also part of the legislation, which are an equally important part of the legislation and should not be sidelined by the participants. the aim should be to achieve a competitive regulatory environment within the eu, while taking account of the needs of businesses and promoting employment, production and innovation. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. however, i am voting against this resolution because i believe that there is a need for a better balance between the present weak legislation and the different privileges enjoyed by the member states, while taking into account the important rules for the protection of workers, consumers and the environment.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report contains many positive elements, in particular in terms of addressing issues such as racism, multiculturalism, tolerance, awareness and understanding of other cultures, and democratic values and human rights. however, the concept of the european dimension in education as raised in the report is rather complex and unclear. therefore, many points in this report are controversial such as the fact that it acknowledges the democratic deficit and the existence of inequalities between and within member states, but makes no specific criticism of the current european policies and practices that have created these realities. it therefore deliberately gives the wrong impression that if citizens are better informed about the eu, then their lives will automatically improve. the structures and practices of the eu and its institutions are not in question here. the european commission is also given a full mandate to interpret the broad concept of the european dimension in education (from the design and implementation of the framework to the identification of best practices of the european dimension in education and private sector/business participation). that is why we voted against.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and stress the growing importance of the european dimension in different disciplines, levels and types of education, while stressing the need for a broad and deep understanding of the concept that takes into account its complex, dynamic and multi-layered nature, with eu learning in school as the central component.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the education is an important tool in educating and informing eu young people, making them active citizens in today's multicultural and complex societies. in view of the contemporary challenges facing the eu, mainstreaming eu curricula in schools can help to restore european citizens' confidence in the union and in the common european vision. the right information for citizens from a young age can reduce the current indifference and alienation towards the functioning of the eu and the phenomenon of euroscepticism. the need for action in the field of education is urgent, as 44% of european citizens feel they have a limited understanding of how the eu works and 52% believe their opinion does not matter, according to a 2014 eurobarometer survey. learning the different aspects of the eu in schools, which include not only economic policy issues but also the social and cultural opportunities the union offers young people, will therefore help to better and more fully understand the principles and values of the eu, such as peaceful coexistence, solidarity and respect for human rights.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report drawn up by the committee on culture and education on the promotion and learning of eu actions in the education systems of the member states and the call by the latter on the eu commission to take a legislative initiative on this matter immediately. the report makes it clear that the eu is going through a period of crisis in terms of its democratic legitimacy, not only because of the lack of knowledge of eu mechanisms among european citizens, but also because of the limitations on their participation in decision-making processes. in an era of intense euroscepticism, the introduction of a more european dimension in education can be a decisive factor in maintaining the cohesion of the eu. the updating of education systems and the adoption of a more european approach to them will also make a decisive contribution to the process of further integration of the union.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report made several positive references to tackling racism and the need to respect and promote human rights. however, the concept of the european dimension in education as it arises is unclear. many of the reports are controversial. for example, while acknowledging the democratic deficit and the existence of inequalities between and within member states, there is no criticism of the european policies and practices that have created these realities. the attempt to link education with employability and to establish a partnership with the private sector is also particularly negative. nor is there any mention of human rights violations in the eu. unfortunately, a number of amendments tabled by the group of the european united left to improve the text were rejected. we therefore abstained.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and note that the adoption of the erasmus+ programme to replace the various previous programmes has been a significant development for the concept of mobility throughout the european higher education area.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report emphasises encouraging and facilitating access to education/training for pupils with a view to enhancing their mobility, and also includes actions aimed at removing cultural barriers and supporting foreign language learning. all disadvantaged groups and people with disabilities should be effectively included in information meetings and preparation. the report also stresses the need to strengthen the recognition of qualifications, certificates and informal learning, and the importance of ensuring a strong learning component in vocational education, training and apprenticeships. it also recognises that mobility should not be the only envisaged solution to the unacceptable dimensions of youth unemployment in the eu. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the european programme erasmus+ promotes the mobility of european students and staff of higher education institutions. this is to ensure parallel exchange of students between the institutions of the union with the ultimate aim of reducing the disparity in unemployment rates between the member states. providing mobility opportunities for young scientists also boosts the productive economy, as young people are able to come into contact with new scientific methods. however, the difficulty of adequate and full recognition of qualifications and traineeships from one country to another and the lack of a programme for the vocational training of young people, as efforts are mainly directed towards higher education, are a problem. i believe that the main positive outcome of all this is, among other things, to increase employment, to spread the values of european citizenship and to improve the education and training of young people in the union. therefore, the present difficulties should be addressed through policies such as the adaptation of education systems to effectively address the skills mismatch problem and the existence of a corresponding policy on vocational and technical training.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report on erasmus+ and other tools to promote mobility in vocational education and training, which clearly shows the need to improve and broaden mobility options for vocational training students. a clear picture of available european programmes combined with available vocational training options will enhance mobility and become an essential tool to help reduce the unemployment rates observed in several member states. on the other hand, i cannot help but note that the commission's legislative initiative in this area cannot ignore the need to protect acquired skills as certified professional experience, and to avoid the phenomena which make employees a cheap labour option.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report is yet another one which aims to further consolidate power in brussels by removing the sovereign right of the member states to be represented in international organizations. the commission is seeking to transfer this representation to brussels. it has been shown that this accumulation of power does not serve the small states and peoples, but instead aims to serve the big ones and especially the big capital. as a group, we have presented a minority view, presenting the basic parameters of our own position, we have therefore voted against the report.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the today's globalisation has led to a significant interdependence of economies around the world and so the various international organisations are exposed to the risk of failure if one organisation fails the other. although these bodies have their own separate governance, i believe that the eu should regulate important aspects of their operation so as to reduce the complexity and divergence in practices and thus mitigate the risk of their failure, a failure which will drag the entire international economic and monetary community along with it, as happened with the recent financial crisis. through shared governance, the eu will also be able to promote the values of its treaties, such as transparency, accountability and democratisation. these principles should clarify the objectives pursued by the establishment of each body, together with its means of operation and sources of funding, including voluntary contributions and donations. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are not undermined by the failure of the member states to comply with the obligations imposed on them by the treaty.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report removes the sovereign right of the member states to be represented in international organizations and instead transfers this right to brussels. in this way, more and more powers are being transferred to the centre of the eu, so that the interests of the large and rich member states can be better represented. we voted against because the interests of the peoples and the interests of small states such as cyprus are not served.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and recall that coastal fishing is an extremely important form of fishing in eu countries. it ensures a high level of employment and is a socially and environmentally sustainable form of fishing.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ despite the general consensus that the common fisheries policy is particularly negative in relation to small-scale fishing, this report is a step in the right direction. whereas it recognises that small-scale fishing should not be categorised in the same way as large-scale fishing and that a clearer distinction should therefore be made; the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the coastal fishing is a well-known form of fishing for many member states of the union. as a form of employment, it is also environmentally friendly. called differently in each country, such as 'traditional' or 'industrial' fishing, it is an important means of livelihood and a way of life for many inhabitants of coastal and island areas. the eu has already adopted several regulations on this sector, such as the common fisheries policy (cfp) regulation of 2013 and the european maritime and fisheries fund (emff) regulation of 2014, taking into account the needs of these coastal areas. these regulations have recognised the need to protect this occupation by measures such as preferential access to 12 nautical miles. the emff also supports innovation and new investment in this area, such as investment in education and information programmes. the recession of recent years, which has been caused by the general economic difficulties in many of these island regions, must therefore be tackled by new ideas and policies. this can be achieved by a new cap, which will provide a more modern framework adapted to the new challenges.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and believe that the new eu cap includes important parameters and, if properly implemented, will improve eu standards for fishing in remote waters. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
