in writing. ‒ we express our dismay and deep concern at the alarmingly increasing rate of deadly attacks on hospitals and schools in armed conflicts around the world, where patients, students, medical and teaching staff, humanitarian aid workers, children and family members are targeted and victimized. we therefore call on the eu and un member states, the eu institutions and the vice-president/high representative to recognise the true extent of this state of emergency and to make use of all the means at their disposal.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the we have supported the joint resolution, which has been co-signed by our own political group. it contains important elements calling, inter alia, for the eu, the un and the international community in general to take serious action for violations of international humanitarian law for attacks on schools and hospitals. our position is that such attacks must be condemned by all, even in war zones. also, those who use protected people or assets as human shields or a vehicle of deception are also guilty of violations of international humanitarian law.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the resolution in question is a confirmation of the need to apply international humanitarian law in cases of armed conflict. it is the common belief of the members of the european parliament that the european executive bodies can and should help to strengthen the efforts of the international humanitarian investigation committee set up under the 1949 geneva convention for the effective application of international humanitarian law. it is regrettable and worthy of reflection that the states participating in the 32nd international conference of the red cross did not agree to define a new mechanism to reinforce the existing system of governance in relation to international humanitarian law. the commission and the council are therefore called upon, through their representatives, to take the lead in strengthening the institutions for the effective application of humanitarian law.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the we voted against the resolution because it rightly expresses deep regret at the lack of respect for international humanitarian law and condemns the murderous attacks on hospitals, schools and other civilian targets which are occurring with increasing frequency. it is also very important that the resolution stresses that international convictions must be accompanied by independent investigations and real accountability. the member states and the eu institutions are therefore called upon to recognise the true extent of this critical situation and to address it.
in writing. ‒ we support the report, as the right of public access to documents is a fundamental right protected by the charter of fundamental rights and the treaties.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the this own-initiative report deals with a very important issue, namely the free and unhindered access of the public to union documents. the right of public access to documents is a fundamental right protected by the charter of fundamental rights and the treaties and in which serious shortcomings have been observed. the report promotes democracy and transparency, the transparency of legislative and administrative procedures and the transparency of the management of the structural funds. we believe that the report is moving in the right direction, although there could be even more forceful remarks. we therefore consider this vote in favour to be appropriate.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the i voted against the motion for a resolution by the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs on public access to documents of the institutions. the resolution calls for the full application of the principle of transparency, with the exceptions provided for in regulation no 1049/2001, by the institutions with a view to eliminating the phenomenon of citizens' lack of access to information held by the representatives of the european union. the content of the resolution shows the urgent need to establish faster, less burdensome and more accessible procedures for dealing with complaints of refusal of access to public documents, while limiting the costly legal disputes that follow.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report criticised the opaque operating methods of the eib, the ecb and other european institutions, as well as the commission's methods of concluding international agreements, and the influence of interest groups in the european institutions. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. ‒ the report contains a number of positive points, but also significant negative points.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report focuses on a sector of the workforce that is largely marginalised in the eu. the report stresses the need for common recognition at eu level of the profession and the value of domestic and care work as real work, as recognition of this profession is likely to reduce undeclared work and promote social inclusion. the report contains many important issues which both the member states and the commission and council should take seriously. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.
in writing. ‒ the according to the international labour organisation's 2010 figures, 2.5 million people were employed as domestic helpers in the eu, 88% of whom were women. if we take into account undeclared work and those living in europe without work permits, we find that the figures are much higher. i supported the report which calls for common recognition at eu level of the profession and value of domestic work and of staff in the care of the elderly and children. the report also highlights the need to promote the exchange of best practices between member states, building on the example of successful models with a positive social and labour impact in the sector such as the 'service note' in belgium, which have proven their effectiveness in reducing undeclared work, improving working conditions and granting legal labour rights to domestic helpers and care staff; it is also important that member states ratify and implement without delay ilo convention no 189 on decent work for domestic workers.
in writing. ‒ we voted in favour of the report as it moves in the right direction to strengthen the labour and human rights and freedoms of women working as domestic helpers and dependent care staff in the eu.

gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. we are particularly concerned about the need to combat gender stereotypes, which are detrimental to the eu's competitiveness. equally problematic is the approach that policies promoted under the digital agenda may contribute to the gender gap in telecommunications. as a party we have repeatedly expressed our concerns about the digital agenda. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to support them. the report calls for the protection of labour rights and the fight against precarious employment. it is also very important that it warns that the flexibility offered by digitalisation can lead to precarious working conditions. the call for support for the un proposals on gender equality and for the development of a comprehensive strategy to tackle inequalities by member states is equally positive. the need to promote immediate measures to address the gender pay gap and to ensure the free nature of the internet and free access for all is also very positive.

the commission has also adopted a communication on the madam president, ladies and gentlemen, today the commission and the mainstream circles in the union are repeating their wishes for economic growth and employment for young people, through the same failed policies of austerity and memorandum. cyprus, despite the celebrations of the 'success story' of the memoranda and austerity policies, is yet another tragic example of this failure. the right-wing government in cyprus and the troika with the memorandum policies have led to a youth unemployment rate of 28.2%. the number of employees has fallen by 30,000 in the last three years, while thousands of young people are moving abroad or not returning home after their studies. cyprus has the highest rate of increase in longterm unemployment in the union, even after the supposed exit from the memorandum. the country was ranked fifth in the european union in terms of parttime employment, with wages of €500 and 94.3% of employees saying that the contract they signed was not their choice. neoliberal austerity policies have failed. we need direct public investment for social development and the creation of new, safe and decent jobs. the speaker accepts to answer a question with a 'blue card' (rule 162 (8))

in writing. ‒ we support the report and consider that the conclusion of a sustainable fisheries partnership agreement with liberia would clearly help the eu to achieve its objectives on combating iuu fishing and extend the common fisheries policy (cfp) objective of sustainable management of fisheries and aquaculture activities.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the european union fisheries partnership agreement (fpa) with liberia forms part of the union's wider policy of providing financial and technical support to foreign states in return for fishing rights. the agreement will be the first between the european union and liberia, whereas cooperation has so far only been possible through the united nations convention on the law of the sea. the union's objectives through this agreement include combating illegal and unregulated (iuu) fishing, which has contributed significantly to the reduction of the national income of liberian fishermen and the destruction of the country's marine ecosystem. the agreement also provides for the establishment of a fisheries agreement with the european union, which will be signed in the framework of the liberian fisheries agreement. in my vote, i support the conclusion of a fisheries partnership agreement with liberia, in the hope that this agreement will be put to good use to help combat illegal fishing, protect marine biodiversity and provide economic and social support for the country.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the through this protocol signed with liberia, the eu is entitled to access liberia's fishing zone in exchange for a lease, specific quantities that european fishermen will be entitled to fish and support for the development of sectoral fisheries policies for the country. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and agree with the rapporteur's position. the new protocol represents a major step forward compared to the previous one and the new commitments made by mauritania in terms of transparency and preferential access for the european fleet are welcomed, taking into account the bad experience of previous years, in which mauritania allowed foreign fleets to fish for cephalopods while denying access to the european fleet.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the new protocol signed between the eu and mauritania in july 2015 is valid for four years and concerns the right of european vessels to operate in mauritian waters. the new protocol provides for an annual eu financial contribution of €55 million, as opposed to €67 million under the previous agreement. a corresponding financial support of eur 16.5 million is provided to promote sustainable development in the fisheries sector and environmental protection in sensitive marine areas. the amount of financial assistance has been increased considerably since the previous agreement provided for an amount of eur 3 million for the same purpose. i therefore share the rapporteur's view that the new protocol is improved in many respects, since it promotes the development of the region by maintaining the 60% quota for black crew members on european vessels. this helps to provide jobs to black citizens while reducing unemployment and poverty in the country. this is very important, considering that mauritania is one of the poorest and least developed countries in the world with 42% of the population living below the poverty line.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the protocol signed with mauritania allows european fishing vessels to enter the country's fishing zone in exchange for a financial contribution, but also requires 60% of the fishing vessel's staff to be mauritanian. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to support the development of the regions concerned.
in writing. ‒ we support the proposal for a council decision on the conclusion of the cooperation agreement between the european community and its member states, of the one part, and the republic of korea, of the other part, on a civilian global navigation satellite system, as we consider the republic of korea to be a country with considerable potential in the field of space technology and its applications.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the as a group, we are opposed to the navigation systems developed by the eu. the reasons are that they are already being used for military purposes despite declarations to the contrary, but also because they do not meet the objectives they set themselves (independence from the american gps). on the contrary, the americans have the right to block the eu's satellite navigation system. we voted against it for the above reasons.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and underline that a bottom-up approach and the involvement of local actors are crucial to achieving the objectives of cohesion policy.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the treaty of lisbon, in article 174, defines territorial cohesion as one of the union's main and fundamental objectives. the commission notes that the aid is granted to the following undertakings: the programming defines two new instruments to contribute to territorial development, namely local development on the initiative of local communities (ldci) and integrated spatial investment (isi). the report states that the cipt will operate at subregional level and will be based on the initiative of local communities, which will be able to cooperate with both private and public bodies. the oct will also contribute to sustainable urban development by pooling funds from the various operational programmes. both territorial development tools face challenges such as the complexity of their implementation, their acceptance by local actors, lack of expertise, possible lack of flexibility and adequate guidance from the commission and finally the increase in administrative burden. i would nevertheless like to vote in favour of the report, believing that with a careful strategy these tools can make a significant contribution to achieving the objective of territorial development within the framework of cohesion policy.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the new cohesion policy framework for the 2014-2020 programming period places greater emphasis on the use of integrated and site-based approaches to promote economic, social and territorial cohesion. to achieve this, two new instruments were introduced for the 2014-2020 programming period: local development on the initiative of local communities (ldci) and integrated spatial investment (isi). i voted in favour of this report, which stresses the importance of the bottom-up approach, since the involvement of local actors is crucial to achieving the objectives of cohesion policy.
in writing. ‒ we support the report as regulation (ec) no 184/2005 on balance of payments (bop), international trade in services (its) and foreign direct investment (fdi) statistics is part of the overall eu framework aimed at monitoring economic developments in individual member states and in the union as a whole. the production of reliable statistics in these fields plays a particularly important role in monitoring vital data such as developments in current accounts and financial flows within and outside the eu.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the regulation (ec) no 184/2005 concerning balance of payments (bop), international trade in services (its) and foreign direct investment (fdi) statistics aims to monitor the economic situation of the member states and of the union itself in general. monitoring financial flows plays a key role in all surveillance in order to ensure the consistency of member states' economic policies and the union's control of the implementation of the guidelines and, ultimately, to ensure the broader macroeconomic policy. increasing capital flows and their increasing complexity have now led to the need for a revision of the regulation with a view to increasing the transparency and level of detail of statistics, adding flexibility to take advantage of modern innovations that can make statistics relating to balance of payments, international trade in services and foreign direct investment more reliable. the regulation also needs to be amended to bring it into line with the provisions of the treaty of lisbon. i support this resolution because i believe that transparency, as well as the level of detail in statistics, are vital tools for the union to ensure proper monitoring and monitoring of economic developments in the member states.

s&d), in writing. ‒ the the marginalisation of the union's mountain regions is a fact, since the vast majority of development policies are directed at lowland areas. i believe, however, that the difficulties which exist, such as the lack of specific infrastructure and the need for more expenditure due to the isolation of these areas, must be overcome if there is to be a systematic and correct exploitation of mountain areas based on their specificities. the natural wealth of mountains, which leads to an abundance of renewable energy sources, can help union policies aimed at protecting the environment. this will create new jobs, the so-called 'green' jobs in the report, which help to combat climate change. cohesion policy in mountain areas will also help to combat the poverty and social exclusion experienced by the inhabitants of these areas, with the ultimate aim of sustainable economic development in the mountains. i would therefore like to vote in favour of the resolution, since i am in favour of the need to draw up an agenda for the formulation of a policy for the union's mountain regions, which combines the various existing indirect policies relating to mountains, with the ultimate aim of achieving a more comprehensive strategy for the development of mountain areas.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report which adopts more targeted and coordinated policies for the eu's mountain regions, which are rarely at the centre of cohesion policy. this report calls for an eu mountain agenda and for the integration of the mountain dimension into future eu programmes and policies, in particular the european structural and investment funds. the eu's mountain regions can contribute to its objectives, such as in 'europe 2020'. the rich natural resources and advantages of mountain areas can create jobs by improving employment within the eu, in particular green jobs that help to combat climate change. it is also important to develop synergies between the various funds and measures (including rural development) so that the specificities of mountain areas are taken into account when preparing and examining operational programmes.

in writing. ‒ we support the report, as we consider it necessary to review an appropriate regime for commodity traders and the prudential supervision of investment firms. this is necessary to ensure the development of a consistent prudential framework for all types of investment firms
s&d), in writing. ‒ the regulation (eu) no 575/2013 on capital requirements applies to both credit institutions and investment firms. articles 493 (1) and 498 (1) of the regulation provide that commodity dealers are exempted from large exposures and own funds requirements. those two exemptions expire in 2017 and a new prudential regulatory framework should be put in place which takes into account the specific circumstances and risks faced by commodity traders in their business. according to the report, the definition includes those who 'only trade commodity derivatives' and those who 'trade commodity derivatives merely as an ancillary activity to commodity production'. the commission has decided to examine and define a new framework for the application of the exemptions for traders and a more general regime for the prudential supervision of investment firms. the complexity of the procedure may delay the completion of the new framework and there is a risk that the existing regulation will continue to apply without the above exemptions. i therefore think it is right to extend the validity of the two exemptions beyond their expiry until the adoption of the new regulatory framework.

in writing. ‒ we support the report as we believe that an integrated approach to the issue of gender equality and reconciliation of work and family life in future eu legislation will bring coherence and transparency to the effort and help promote gender-based reconciliation of work and family life.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the the parental leave directive (96/34/eu) was originally adopted in 1996 and adapted in 2010 (2010/18/eu) following a new agreement between member states. however, there is a need to modernise it, since it does not correspond in principle to the present social and economic reality, and there are also divergent practices in the member states, which causes a lack of uniformity in its application. although the commission has not taken the initiative to evaluate the parental leave directive, the eurofound executive agency, together with researchers and experts from the member states, has produced an evaluation report. the study concluded that there is a mismatch in the use of parental leave compared with the other two types of leave available, namely maternity leave and paternity leave. the use of parental leave by fathers was also found to be limited, as there is a reduced remuneration during the period. for the above reasons, i would like to vote in favour of this report, since i agree that the directive should be revised in terms of duration, remuneration and scope. i also agree that there should be a directive on paternity leave, which has not yet been done.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the parental leave not only contributes to gender equality and women's access to employment but also allows both parents to meet their obligations towards their children. however, in recent years only 2.7% of fathers have taken parental leave. in addition, the use of parental leave varies from country to country. i voted in favour of the report by mrs maria arena, which calls for the framework agreement on parental leave to be modernised and improved. we ask the commission to extend the minimum duration of parental leave from four to at least six months in order to improve the balance between family and professional life. parental leave should be an opportunity, not a hindrance.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ we voted against the report as it contains very positive elements regarding the extension of parental leave.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and call on member states to introduce strict criminal sanctions for the crimes of trafficking in human beings, modern slavery and human exploitation. in addition, we stress that prevention is crucial to tackling trafficking in human beings and that is why member states must ensure that they make every effort to ensure that there are no victims in the first place.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the trafficking in human beings, regardless of gender, is a flagrant violation of the fundamental rights and human dignity of victims (article 5 (3) of the eu charter of fundamental rights). victims of such acts suffer irreparable damage that often follows them for life. the fight against the crime of trafficking in human beings must be a primary concern of the union through targeted policies that are implemented immediately and in practice. the need for immediate action has also been highlighted by the current refugee crisis, which has given impetus to such illegal actions. it also highlighted the lack of appropriate tools on the part of the union to tackle and eliminate this crime. this resolution will contribute positively to improving the policy response to trafficking in human beings by advocating the immediate implementation of directive 2011/36/eu, which contains provisions concerning both the fight against and the protection of victims. special attention is given to women victims of trafficking in human beings, who account for 80% of the total number, and to strengthening their support services. finally, the directive requires the commission to submit reports assessing the measures taken by states to combat trafficking in human beings and the criminalisation of the use of services which are the object of trafficking in human beings.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the over 30,000 victims of trafficking in human beings were recorded in the eu between 2010 and 2012, 80% of whom were women. children account for about 16 percent of recorded victims, and girls for 13 percent. women and girls account for up to 95% of victims of sexual exploitation, while the majority of victims of labour exploitation are men (71%). in addition, with the "explosion" of migration flows, according to europol data, around 10,000 unaccompanied children have disappeared since arriving in the eu in 2015. it has been found that european legislation on the protection of victims of trafficking in human beings is not being applied properly. i voted in favour of the report calling on the member states to step up efforts to protect victims of trafficking in human beings and to take measures to prevent and provide assistance and support. vulnerable victims must be identified quickly and given the necessary support.

s&d), in writing. ‒ the poverty and social exclusion are today serious social problems which must be tackled on a pan-european basis. the eu must provide the regulatory framework and initiatives so that we move in the right direction. the increase in the proportion of people living below the poverty line is worrying and requires us to act immediately. it is also a fact that women bear the consequences of poverty more than men, especially if they are single mothers or disabled. this is also reflected in the occupational sector, where there is a wage gap, as women try to combine family and professional obligations. the resolution is an initiative against inequality against women in areas such as employment, social security and pay. i would like to vote in favour of this initiative to contribute to the effort to achieve social justice. i think it's also a broader way of reducing global poverty in a broader context.
s&d), in writing. ‒ the today, 122 million people in europe are at risk of poverty. women, like children, are among the most vulnerable groups to fall into poverty. the number of women in poverty has consistently exceeded that of men, with some 65 million women currently living in poverty. the economic crisis and austerity policies have led to an increase in the gender gap in the labour market, with women being hit harder. in addition, gender discrimination in the labour market of the member states, covering various aspects such as employment, remuneration, social benefits and pensions, is one of the root causes of women being at higher risk of poverty. i voted in favour of the report because it contains a number of measures to combat social exclusion and gender inequality, which lead to poverty for women. in particular, measures are needed to promote work-life balance, high-quality public services to combat poverty, especially women's poverty, and the introduction of a child guarantee which ensures free healthcare, free education, decent housing and adequate nutrition for every child in europe.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report rightly points out that the economic crisis has affected women more, particularly when they are pensioners, immigrants or disabled. it is also rightly pointed out that women still hold the majority of precarious and parttime jobs. both the gender pay gap and the pension gap burden women who face economic difficulties and cause daily uncertainty and impoverishment. it is a fact that neoliberal austerity policies towards workers, such as wage cuts and benefit cuts, hit women twice as hard, who statistically experience poverty more than men. while women are thus subject to the double exploitation of this system (as workers and as women), the economic crisis is leading to a further deepening of class differences and widening of inequalities. the report highlights the continuing inequalities affecting women and urges the member states and the commission to work towards providing equal opportunities and opportunities for all in the areas of work, social security, pay, education and childcare and to stop the impoverishment of people. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.

s&d), in writing. ‒ the it is not disputed that the single market has been achieved through effective cooperation between the member states of the union. the removal of tariff barriers to trade is also an integral part of the single market. in contrast, non-tariff barriers have not yet been removed, which creates a heterogeneous pattern of state practices and the union thus lacks a common policy in this area. it is reasonable and understandable that there cannot be a complete abolition of non-tariff duties, since under the basic principle of subsidiarity, member states have the first say on matters which do not constitute european primary law. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. however, the aim of removing many non-tariff barriers is to reduce the protective measures by member states so as to open up domestic markets even more and to benefit businesses across europe, as well as consumers, from lower prices. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
gue/ngl), in writing. ‒ the the report calls for the complete abolition of tariff barriers to strengthen and complete the single market. in addition to our concerns about the policies and objectives served by the single market, we must also recognise that certain tariff barriers are particularly important. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be able to apply the same rules to all types of goods and services, including those which are not covered by the directive. the barriers to genetically modified products are also important. we therefore voted against the report because we disagree with the general framework in which tariff barriers are managed and with the objectives served by the abolition of tariff barriers in the single market. we also voted against because we think it is important to respect the specificities of the member states which lead to the creation of certain barriers.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented. madam president, i would like to ask a question. it has been said that we must be even more ambitious. my question is very simple: we are not the united states of europe as the united states of america is. in the united states of america the states have the right to determine their tax policy. i think you will agree with me that if we want to be effective, let's stick to the oecd agreed basis for beps. if you want to go beyond that, i think we're entering very uncharted waters. do you agree or disagree ?
the commission i think that we are all against tax evasion, against tax avoidance, and that we are all committed to fighting tax havens. but i think, listening to the discussions, that in our overzealous effort to combat tax evasion and tax avoidance, we may actually end up backfiring on the european union itself, and instead of blacklists being created and companies avoiding tax havens, they will end up avoiding the european union itself. we'll end up hurting our own businesses. in the end, we will be hurting the same consumers, the same citizens, the same gnp of our countries. we must be very careful. i hear about tax equalization and flat tax rates. these were applied to centralized systems in the former communist countries. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. what will the companies do? they will all leave the european union, they will go to other countries and we will in effect be putting the united states of america, the third countries outside the european union and their tax havens in a very favourable position. we are essentially going to be blacklisted in our attempt to crack down on tax evasion and tax avoidance. i think we should be clear: i would like to say that the commission has been very careful in its approach to the problem of the taxation of capital, and that it has been very careful in its approach to the problem of the taxation of capital. and we must say this: the european union has the strictest rules on tax transparency, on its control systems, which are not shared by other competing countries. we must not under any circumstances undermine the competitiveness of financial services in the european union. just as we have some countries that are strong in industry, we have some other countries that are strong financial centers. well, we're not going to hit them; to please who?
s&d), in writing. in the united states of america each state sets its own tax practices. the eu is made up of member states with the right to formulate their own tax policy, which we ask them to harmonise so that we have the characteristics of a federal state. if we were the united states of europe, we would have implemented many other things before we studied common tax practices. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women in matters of employment, vocational training and promotion, and working conditions, which was adopted by the council on 14 june, is designed to ensure that the right to equal treatment for men and women is guaranteed in all member states. such advanced measures are the so-called switch over clause for non-exemption from taxation of income from the sale of shares in a third country and exit taxation which provides that when the tax residence of a company is transferred from one country to another, tax should be paid in proportion to the fair market value. the directive essentially abolishes the comparative advantage of member states in the context of free competition and operates to the detriment of small countries which have for decades built their economies by creating a related comparative advantage, without even proposing a compensatory framework.

in writing. ‒ the in 2005, the first global framework convention on tobacco trade control (fctc) was signed by the world health organisation with 180 contracting states, including the eu. the protocol to eliminate illicit trade in tobacco products was signed in 2012 under the authority of article 15 of the fctc as part of this wider convention. the protocol has not yet entered into force, since it requires the signature of at least 40 states to enter into force. i believe it is our duty to combat the illicit trade in tobacco and cigarettes in the union, which endangers the health of european citizens. at the same time, by fighting the illicit trade in tobacco, we are also helping to combat tax evasion, since the union loses a large part of its revenue from non-payment of taxes by tobacco traders. since much of the illicit tobacco trade is carried out by non-eu countries, a global control regime is also the best and most effective solution to eliminate this phenomenon. i therefore vote against this recommendation, as i believe that the eu should sign the protocol to eradicate illicit tobacco trade as soon as possible so that it can be implemented immediately.

in writing. ‒ the the european commission's report on policy coherence for development, published last august, addressed the 2030 agenda for sustainable development, of which policy coherence is a part. policy cohesion for development (pcd) is an eu initiative which has yet to be extended internationally. it is based mainly on the idea that developed countries should be friendly to developing countries and not introduce policies that are hostile to them. the aim must be to spread such policies internationally. as the subject is purely political, there should be an effort to cooperate with joint planning and coordination on the part of the union so that recommendations on sps issues are introduced directly by the member states. the 2015 report is a positive step in the right direction to improve legislation on the subject. at the same time, new tools for promoting the cap, such as impact assessments of development policies, have begun to be developed. in the absence of this initiative, i regard trade, security and immigration as a priority area of the cap, with the ultimate objective of stabilising the union's neighbouring countries.

in writing. ‒ the although the report contained some positive elements, its one-dimensional approach to the issue of technological solutions for sustainable agriculture in the eu did not allow us to support it. the technological solutions introduced in recent decades have undoubtedly lowered production costs and therefore increased production. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not disrupted by the crisis. the report also fails to address the burning issue of food waste, which is estimated to account for 30% of production. we have therefore voted against the report.

in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report because we fundamentally disagree with the commission's approach. we believe that the current agricultural crisis is very closely linked to the environmental, social and economic crisis, to a globalised production-consumption system and to the unequal distribution of resources in the eu and around the world. new techniques and varieties together with new technologies are increasing even more the dependence on multinationals and banks thus widening the gap between the large industrialized food producers and the small scale farmers. and so along with their marginalization, local varieties are being lost along with the cultural heritage associated with traditional harvesting methods.
the commission has also adopted a communication on the mr president, ladies and gentlemen, at the moment when we are talking about a new immigration agreement, the situation is getting worse instead of better. every day innocent people, including many children and infants, are sentenced to death. in the last week of may alone, 900 refugees died of cold in the mediterranean. the situation is shameful and unacceptable. the official policy of the union cannot continue to undermine its declared principles of solidarity, to close the borders, to promote deportations, to sign shameful agreements with turkey on refugees. the situation is tragic. even people rescued from the multi-neck shipwrecks face squalid living conditions, with hotspots now being turned into concentration camps. to stop crime, we must stop empty promises and take immediate action. immediately establish a common and fair asylum system based on practical solidarity and equal burden-sharing. establish legal and safe pathways and a permanent, binding and fair system for the status of refugees. it is equally important that they become human and dignified reception spaces for refugees both in the union and in third countries.
s & d) and the mr president, the success of the new resettlement pact does not lie in the adoption of new measures to deal with the refugee crisis but in its faithful implementation without derogations, otherwise we will be exposed again as europeans, as with the implementation of the resettlement programme, where national egos have been overcome. solidarity, equal burden-sharing, adherence to the principles and values of the european union, respect for human rights, emphasis on legal channels of migration, cooperation between the european union and third countries of origin and transit, an adequate and sustainable financial base. these are the basic elements that must govern the new pact. there must be a common management of the external borders, which will guarantee security while fighting criminal activities. however, border management does not imply border closure.

in writing. ‒ the the 2010 protocol to the international convention on civil liability and compensation for damage in connection with the carriage of hazardous and noxious substances by sea needs to be ratified by the member states separately on behalf of the eu. the original international convention on this subject was signed in 1996 and deals with issues of liability and compensation for damage caused by harmful substances such as liquefied natural gas and liquefied gas. this objective liability shall be borne by the shipowner who carries the dangerous substance. there is also mention of the establishment of a special compensation fund to be paid to any person who has suffered damage as a result of the carriage of weee by sea. given that shipping is an international business activity with cross-border implications, an international, rather than a european, regime with uniform application should be ensured so that such accidents can be dealt with more effectively and fairly. i would like to vote in favour of this resolution, so that we can move in that direction.

in writing. ‒ the the free movement of european citizens has been a major objective of the eu for many years, and new measures are constantly being taken to improve and facilitate citizens in this area as much as possible. this report concerns the simplification of the official acceptance of certain public documents in the eu. it is remarkable that we have now succeeded in getting some 12 million european citizens to live, study and work in another member state than their country of origin. the regulation in question is aimed at simplifying the registration of other identity documents relating to nationality, residence and criminal records. it also concerns facilitation in relation to documents requiring official translation for their authentication. the regulation also provides for a safety net by enabling the authorities to verify the authenticity of the document directly by the issuing authority by means of electronic communication. an interesting initiative in this area is the introduction of multilingual standard forms for these public documents. i would therefore like to vote in favour of this resolution to achieve easy and safe movement of european citizens within the union.
in writing. ‒ i supported a relevant legislative resolution on promoting the free movement of citizens by simplifying the requirements for the submission of certain public documents in the european union and amending regulation (eu) no 1024/2012. as described in the text of that resolution, the mobility of union citizens will be enhanced by this amendment, which provides for the exemption from the obligation to validate and mark (the 'hague seal'), two administrative formalities normally required for public documents issued by another state. measures to facilitate acceptance are also provided for certified photocopies and official translations. these facilities for the citizen are offset by the possibility for the authority receiving the document to ask the issuing authority directly and by electronic means to verify the authenticity of the document.
in writing. ‒ the the aim of the regulation is to simplify substantially the official acceptance of certain public documents in the eu, due to the cross-border mobility of citizens. at present, authentication and notation of public documents issued by another state is required. the introduction of a regulation will relieve these administrative formalities in respect of registration documents, documents relating to residence, nationality and criminal records. these facilities are offset by the possibility for the authorities receiving the document to request a direct and electronic certification of its authenticity from the authority which issued it. there is no political problem with this proposal. that is why we voted in favour.
in writing. ‒ we support the report as we consider that the technical amendments contained in it constitute an improvement of the legislation.
in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report because its general approach has resulted (in the past) in the closure of the special tribunal for civil servants and the transfer of its activities to the general court of the european union. therefore, we do not see the need to hire more judges.
in writing. ‒ the recent reforms of the general court of the european union have increased the number of judges from 28 to 56, so that there are two judges per member state. this increase also requires the abolition of the civil service tribunal and the reinstatement of the general court as the forum for the settlement of disputes between the union and its officials. i therefore think it is reasonable to introduce transitional arrangements to make this readjustment of the general court possible. this motion for a resolution on the transfer to the general court of the eu of jurisdiction to hear at first instance disputes between the union and its officials (public administration disputes) contains both the necessary technical provisions and amendments which improve the existing legislation on this subject, by setting out the conditions for the transfer of jurisdiction and the reforms to the internal organisation of the general court to ensure coherence and efficiency. i would therefore like to vote in favour of this resolution.
in writing. ‒ the i could not help but support the proposed amended draft regulation of the european parliament and of the council transferring to the general court of the european union the jurisdiction to hear at first instance disputes between the union and its officials. following the previous amendment of that regulation to extend the powers of the general court to include the adjudication of cases concerning officials of the union, the abolition of references to the former judicial body, namely the civil service tribunal, is purely supplementary.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and underline that eu citizens have the right to expect a high level of transparency, efficiency, prompt execution and responsiveness from the union's institutions and other institutions and bodies. they also have the right to be adequately informed if they can take further action on their matter.
in writing. ‒ the this motion for a resolution seeks to strengthen the citizen's right to transparency, efficiency, prompt implementation and response by the union institutions and other institutions and bodies, as it finds that the existing range of procedures has gaps and inconsistencies in those procedures. it also contains a regulation on the proper functioning of the institutions, which was drawn up after a group of lawyers had been consulted. it is essentially a continuation of the berlingue report, which was adopted in 2013 and which we voted in favour of.
in writing. ‒ the i welcome the adoption of this regulation, which will bind and govern the action of the institutions and which is a corollary of the union's obligations under the lisbon treaty. european administrative procedural law has its own binding written text which responds to the requirement of sound and efficient administration. the right to be heard, the right to appeal to administrative files and the enhancement of the right to effective appeal against decisions of the union administration are among the rights conferred on union officials by that regulation.
in writing. ‒ this motion for a resolution is about strengthening the citizen's right to transparency, efficiency, prompt execution and response by the union institutions and other bodies and agencies, as it finds that the existing range of procedures has gaps and inconsistencies in those procedures. it also contains a regulation on the proper functioning of the institutions, which was drawn up after a group of lawyers had been consulted. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.

in writing. ‒ the the main problem facing the railway equipment industry, which is not mentioned in the resolution, is the neoliberal programme it is called upon to implement to boost competitiveness and liberalise the market, objectives which are promoted at the expense of policies for growth, support for the real economy and the creation of new quality jobs. the resolution contains some problematic negative references which we cannot ignore. for example, the liberalization of the sector, which is being promoted in the resolution, is inextricably linked to deindustrialization, the geographical transfer of production to the benefit of multinationals and the loss of jobs. the liberalization and privatisation strategies, which are the main factor in the decline of the railway equipment industry in europe, are leading to mass layoffs of workers and the closure of many industrial units. the resolution also omits important references that should have been included if the objective was to support the railway equipment industry. for example, reference should be made to the need to promote public investment and to use programmes such as 'horizon 2020' and 'connecting europe' to fund research in the field of rail technology. the need to promote measures to facilitate access to finance for smes in the railway sector should also be emphasised. we voted against it for these reasons.

in writing. ‒ the the tuna is now protected by many international treaties and by the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas (iccat). the main responsibility of this committee, to which the eu is a party, is to take decisions on the enhancement and conservation of atlantic fisheries resources, including tuna. the timetable for taking action in this direction is until 2022 with objectives such as the replenishment of stocks through the gradual reduction of the allowable fisheries, including tuna. the commission has already succeeded in reducing the total allowable catches, which has led to an increase in tuna stocks. the fishermen themselves have contributed to this result by complying with the commission's decisions. i would therefore like to vote in favour of this resolution, since it seeks to incorporate technical measures into a european regulation, so as to ensure uniform and effective application of commission decisions throughout the european union.

in writing. ‒ we support the report as we believe that the proposal for a regulation on matrimonial property regimes under consideration is clearly in the interest of the union and of international couples covered by it.
in writing. ‒ we voted in favour of the report, which helps international couples within the european union with jurisdiction, applicable law and the recognition and enforcement of decisions in matters of matrimonial property regimes.
in writing. ‒ the the regulation on international couples concerns international jurisdiction, the applicable law, the recognition and enforcement of decisions in the field of registered couples' assets. although family law matters are primarily regulated at national level, the above-mentioned issues are regulated by this regulation. as regards jurisdiction, the regulation lays down the court with jurisdiction to hear disputes concerning the property of spouses. as regards the applicable law, this regulation brings unity, since the competent court will have jurisdiction over all assets irrespective of the location of the property and there will no longer be a separation of the applicable law for each asset separately. finally, as regards recognition, enforceability and enforcement of judgments, a judgment is enforceable only after it has been declared enforceable by the member state concerned, which is obliged to do so unless there is an overriding reason of public policy. i would like to vote in favour of this resolution, considering that it is a way of facilitating international couples by simplifying and consolidating the legislation concerning them.

in writing. ‒ the the report explicitly notes the need to make the obligation to respect human rights, which must be universal and monitored continuously, legally binding when the eu concludes international trade agreements. it also called for the inclusion of a legally binding human rights clause in the current trade negotiations with eu partners, in particular those concerning ttip. the report also recalls the proposal to grant the international labour organisation official observer status at the world trade organisation and to set up a committee on trade and decent work. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.
in writing. we support the report, as we believe that an effective, integrated and dynamic trade and investment policy is of fundamental importance for the european union.
in writing. ‒ the the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to accept them. the commission has not yet adopted the proposal. the report follows the trend with regard to free trade agreements. the report's positive aspects include calls for a greater role for parliament and for impact analyses of the possible impact of such agreements, but no criticism of the policies pursued and their impact on citizens. we have therefore voted against the report.
in writing. we support the report and stress that trafficking in human beings is a modern form of slavery and a serious crime which constitutes one of the worst forms of human rights violations and cannot be tolerated in societies based on respect for human rights, including gender equality.
in writing. ‒ the the report contains very important elements and proposals for dealing with trafficking in human beings. a broad analysis is made of what human trafficking is, what forms it takes and specifically which categories of the population are most vulnerable. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to support the development of the regions concerned. the report calls on the commission and the external action service to raise this issue in the context of the union's external action and to seek international cooperation to combat the phenomenon and to support victims of trafficking. however, the references to operation sophia, which we as a group disagree with, and to measures to strengthen the borders and controls, force us to abstain.
in writing. ‒ the report takes a very clear stand against human trafficking in all its forms: sexual exploitation, forced labour, forced surrogacy, forced begging, torture and extortion, organ removal, etc. the numbers are staggering as an estimated 35 million people worldwide are victims and the annual profits of the illegal human trafficking trade are hundreds of billions of euros. closer international cooperation, exchange of information, not only on fighting human trafficking itself, but also in recovering the illegal profits made are important elements towards fighting human trafficking. the development in a growing number of member states to criminalise the knowing use of sexual services of trafficked persons is very important in order to fight human trafficking.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. madam president, we have said this too many times. we agree to tax transparency, we agree to fair tax competition, we agree to stamp out those real tax havens. i believe, however, that with the intense overzealousness that is being shown, the european union will eventually destroy its own financial centres and drive all companies, both domestic and foreign, to other tax havens and other countries, where the european union will be unable to exercise any control. we are in favour of fair taxation and fair taxation and social justice. but for all this to happen, there have to be companies. if all these companies cannot be taxed and they leave our countries, and we give them away to countries outside the european union, at the end of the day, all we get is talk of blacklists, it's... the commission the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat tax evasion and tax avoidance. none of us are in favour of tax havens and we must fight them. but we must admit: firstly, the european union has the toughest and most stringent tax framework in its countries, but also the strictest framework in transparency, a framework that other countries, and particularly america, do not have. secondly, and this is important, each country in the european union has the possibility and the freedom to have its own tax rates, but not to avoid or evade taxes. having your own tax rate doesn't mean you're cheating on your taxes, you're just doing it to be strong and powerful in the competition. i believe that with the current trend, with extreme positions and positions that actually go to the point of wanting to impose a common corporate tax rate, what we will destroy is our own business centres, our own financial centres and we will actually make and end ...
the commission mr president, despite all the pessimism, suspicion and vacillation, the european union can be made better. i will speak about some issues that can increase the wavering confidence of small states in brussels, and i certainly welcome the work of the commission and the member states, who are actively promoting the war against tax evasion. but i think that the proposal for a single corporate tax rate is contrary to the practice of free competition and does not in any way combat tax evasion, while with the increase in corporate tax most companies will be diverted outside the european union with disastrous consequences for small states, and i am referring to the republic of cyprus. already, london is cutting corporate tax from 20% to 15%. on migration, the commission should closely monitor the implementation of the eu-turkey agreement and should not proceed to visa waiver for turkish passports if turkey does not meet all the criteria. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures to ensure the proper functioning of the internal market. there must also be a mechanism for automatically suspending the visa waiver.

in writing. we support the report and consider that the security of network and information systems is key to european security.
in writing. ‒ the network and information security (nis) is becoming increasingly important for the economy and society. the ida is also a prerequisite for a credible environment for trade in services on a global scale. information systems may be affected by security-threatening events such as human error, natural phenomena, technical failures or malicious attacks. these events are getting bigger, more complex, and occurring more frequently. the absence of ipas can put at risk vital services that depend on the integrity of network and information systems. this may lead to the closure of businesses, cause significant economic damage to the economy and adversely affect social welfare.

the commission has also adopted a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. it is our firm belief that small and mediumsized enterprises (smes) are the backbone of the european economy. they represent more than 90% of all european enterprises and employ two thirds of the workforce in europe. it is clear that the development of smes is a crucial prerequisite for boosting europe's competitiveness and attracting investment and production units. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. for the period 2007-2013, with the support of cohesion policy, €70 billion was allocated to support companies, especially smes, and we welcome the target of doubling this amount for the period 2014-2020.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the first report by the committee of inquiry, ladies and gentlemen, is confined to describing the work programme and does not make any political judgments, since the inquiry is still ongoing. but that does not mean that the first conclusions have not already been drawn. on the contrary, after hours of hearings and study of internal documents, it is now clear that there are serious political responsibilities. both the 2010 estimates and the 2013 study by the commission's joint research centre confirmed that there was a possibility of measurement bias. and yet, no action was taken. the commission was aware of the possibility of fraud but did nothing on the pretext that it was not aware of specific cases. the member states, although informed, also did not carry out any checks. moreover, while it was known that the emissions measurements are much higher under real conditions than the values recorded in laboratories, it was not requested to carry out checks only under real road conditions. there were therefore significant failures on the part of both the commission and the member states in the application of union law and the imposition of sanctions. but let's call it what it is: the gaps in legislation, the strong pressure from the car industry and the safety net of multinational companies have led to the political scandal over emissions measurements. because the volkswagen fraud is not an isolated case. we, colleagues, will continue the investigation and apply further pressure. we will always cooperate constructively with the rapporteurs and the shadow rapporteurs to get the result. we want to put an end to all practices that seek to make unfair profits at the expense of public health and the environment.

in writing. in 1990, interreg was developed as a community initiative with a budget of just eur 1 billion covering crossborder cooperation only and with 11 member states as beneficiaries. interreg was later extended to transnational and interregional cooperation. the commission has also adopted a specific regulation covering the esi funds with erdf support for the period 2014-2020. the esf currently has 28 beneficiary member states and is allocated to one of the two objectives of cohesion policy. we voted against the report because it guarantees: - improved regional connectivity, mobility and accessibility to services; - transfer of knowledge and innovation, enhancing the competitiveness of smes; - addressing environmental challenges, better risk management and disaster response; - enhancing institutional capacity and social capital development, consolidating cross-border network structures and creating new partnerships, and also contributing to territorial cohesion.
in writing. ‒ the i am voting in favour of european territorial cooperation (etc) because it strengthens the economic, social and territorial potential of the regions and leads to crossborder and transnational cooperation, since it integrates different sectoral policies across national borders. it also promotes safety and helps to overcome barriers related to education, health care, transport and energy. the eia is undoubtedly achieving best practices in areas such as better regional connectivity, mobility and accessibility to services, knowledge and innovation transfer, in addressing many environmental challenges through the application of environmental best practices. finally, the esf supports research and innovation as research processes can be carried out on a larger scale. for all these reasons, i am voting in favour of this resolution.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report on european territorial cooperation (etc) because it highlights the importance of the etc as an important instrument of planning cohesion policy to solve problems that transcend administrative boundaries and require common solutions. the esf contributes significantly to strengthening the economic, social and territorial potential of the regions, as it reflects and integrates different sectoral policies at a territorial scale, transcending national borders (cross-border and transnational cooperation), as well as in individual cities and regions (interregional cooperation). despite the small budget, the esi projects have tangible results: removing barriers to better safety, transferring knowledge and innovation, improving regional connectivity, boosting the competitiveness of smes and tackling environmental challenges. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of this resolution.

in writing. ‒ the the redesign of the eu energy market is a major issue for both the union and its member states. in order to achieve energy policy objectives, it is important to facilitate the use of new technologies and to invest, in particular in renewable energy sources and low-carbon power plants. the energy challenges facing europe can be addressed through better integration and coordination of energy markets, providing many opportunities to achieve the common european objectives of a more secure, cost-effective and environmentally sustainable energy supply. i have therefore voted in favour of this report.
in writing. ‒ the the abolition of national policies and the strengthening of the commission's powers to unify the market and create a pan-european electricity market, as proposed in the report, will have disastrous consequences for consumers. the big companies will be the only ones to benefit, as they will be able to operate freely and increase their profits. we are clear that energy must be safeguarded as a public social good, to which we all have equal and cheap access. equally important is that the provision of energy to citizens is a matter of national policy, defined on the basis of the needs of the country and the people, always with a view to social development. finally, the design of the electricity market should be based on a comprehensive proposal to support and develop renewable energy sources, which is also missing from the report. we therefore voted against the report because its sole objective is to make energy a tool for strengthening private interests and the competitiveness of the eu.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and recall that in the mediterranean countries of europe, cooling is a matter of growing concern, and is linked to the gradual climatic increase in temperature. appropriate architectural solutions, insulation and reversible heat pumps - which draw heat from a room in hot weather and channel it when heating is required - can play an important role in solving this problem.
in writing. ‒ the the report makes a number of positive suggestions concerning the promotion of renewable energy sources, the need to combat energy poverty and support vulnerable groups, the increase in funding for housing renovation and for households outside areas with central heating and cooling systems. however, there is a problematic assessment that the key to fighting energy poverty is to increase energy efficiency to reduce overall costs. the proposal seeks to disorient and silence the main cause of energy poverty, which is the neoliberal policies of severe austerity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them.
in writing. ‒ the the issue of heating and cooling is more topical in some member states than in others. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not affected by the effects of the climate change. solutions such as proper building architecture and insulation, and reversible heat pumps, could play an important role in solving the problem. i am voting against the eu heating and cooling strategy because it is an integral part of the energy union strategy and of achieving the eu's 2020, 2030 and 2050 energy and climate goals. it should also be noted that the amount of heat produced by the various industries is sufficient to cover all the heating needs of residential buildings. in addition, i am in favour of the eu's heating and cooling strategy as it provides significant opportunities for eu businesses and consumers if implemented properly. consumers should be at the heart of this strategy through the use of modern technologies and innovative solutions.
in writing. ‒ the the energy union strategy promises to provide all europeans with secure, sustainable and competitive energy, which requires a fundamental change in our energy landscape. among the package of measures to achieve this is the eu's heating and cooling strategy. heating and cooling buildings and industry consume half of the eu's energy. this not only means consuming more energy than is needed, but also means a financial burden for many families in europe. the proposed heating and cooling strategy focuses on removing the barriers to decarbonisation of buildings and industry. increasing energy efficiency and the use of renewable energy sources will also have an impact on the security of energy supply. i voted in favour of the report which calls for ambitious reforms in the energy sector to make it more efficient, environmentally responsible and socially oriented. there is an urgent need to take measures to reduce co2 emissions and avoid a dangerous global warming. this is why the eu should set a binding target of reducing energy consumption by 40% by 2030.

madam president, in its report on exploiting the potential of small and mediumsized enterprises to create new jobs, the european parliament again invokes the free market as a "cure-all". it focuses on reducing so-called labour costs to promote new jobs in smes, and on labour market flexibility. we are not ignoring the good intentions of the rapporteurs and the improvements made to the text. the reference to ensuring the protection of workers, to any increase in flexibility and to supporting the development of cooperative enterprises is agreed. but we disagree with the general philosophy of the report. the general framework proposed for using small and mediumsized enterprises to create jobs does not guarantee the creation of new decent and permanent jobs. nor is there any mention of collective labour rights, i.e. social security, collective bargaining and collective agreements. the 'medicine' of neoliberal austerity policies and labour deregulation for 'supposed' economic growth has failed miserably, as statistics show that small and medium-sized enterprises have declined in recent years in the union.

on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, the eradication of hunger and malnutrition is a major commitment of the european union. our intention must be to achieve sustainable development within objective ii, but also the related objectives, which aim to eliminate both hunger and malnutrition by 2030. however, the current situation proves that our policies so far have been ineffective and wrong, and i disagree with the encouraging results and figures that have been reported. the new targets can be achieved mainly by increasing the productivity of small farms and holdings and by creating sustainable agricultural and food production systems capable of feeding a total population expected to reach 8.5 billion people. it is therefore of particular importance that coordinated action be taken between the global community and national and local authorities, non-governmental organisations and the private sector, as well as between donors, to address hunger and all forms of malnutrition through increased production and a more efficient process of developing the productive food chain. on this basis too, the european union should take into account the need for greater coherence and coordination between all the institutions and bodies of the european union and the member states. member states should, in line with their commitments, step up their efforts for global food security and give priority to sustainable agriculture and local food production, as well as support for smallholder farmers and women farmers as food producers. at the same time, the international community and the european union must work with all countries to support the design and implementation of a comprehensive framework of concrete, feasible and robust national and transnational food measures, based on the sustainable development goals, to reduce underdevelopment and malnutrition. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. finally, humanitarian aid must be targeted, not exploited by corrupt actors, avoided waste and complemented by commission strategies linking humanitarian and development aid. at the same time, the commission should increase investment in specific interventions relating to nutrition, especially in view of the current huge funding gap. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the resolution by which the european parliament gives its consent to ratify the paris agreement to combat climate change. the entry into force of the agreement less than a year after it was signed is a huge achievement, given that it took eight years for the kyoto protocol to enter into force. the european union, which accounts for around 12% of global greenhouse gas emissions, is confirming its leading role in the fight against climate change. together we are now moving towards the eu's commitment to reduce emissions in the european union by at least 40% by 2030. thanks to eu ratification, the paris agreement will enter into force in time for the cop 22 climate change conference in marrakesh.
