in writing. ‒ we support the report and consider the commission's proposal to be a positive step towards ensuring a minimum level of legal aid in all member states.
in writing. ‒ the i vote against the proposal for a directive of the european parliament and of the council on temporary legal aid for suspects or accused persons deprived of their liberty and on legal aid in european arrest warrant proceedings because the right to a fair trial is included and recognised, inter alia, in article 47 of the charter of fundamental rights of the eu and also in article 6 of the european convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. moreover, the scope of the directive should include the right to both interim and ordinary legal aid. i also believe that it is essential that the accused should have legal certainty as regards the eligibility criteria.
in writing. ‒ the the proposed agreement will establish common minimum rules on legal assistance to suspects and accused persons in criminal proceedings, as well as to persons subject to a european arrest warrant, with a view to enhancing mutual trust and therefore mutual recognition in criminal matters. the directive will complete the existing package of procedural rights, which have been and are essential components in creating an area of freedom, security and justice. the extension of the scope to cover all types of legal aid in criminal proceedings, and not just 'temporary' legal aid, is a victory. moreover, the right of persons subject to a european arrest warrant to have access to legal assistance from a lawyer in both the issuing and executing member states is undoubtedly of paramount importance. i have voted in favour of this report for all these reasons.
in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report on this directive because, in addition to the general assistance and legal aid provided to suspects, accused persons deprived of their liberty and wanted persons in the process of executing a european arrest warrant, certain criteria are proposed for the quality of the assistance to be provided. the establishment of common minimum standards to safeguard one of the most basic rights of suspects or accused persons will enhance mutual trust between judicial authorities and thus facilitate the application of the principle of mutual recognition.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and consider that the 'regulation against torture' (ec) no 1236/2005 is a unique instrument that contributes to the protection of human rights through controls of the items and activities that are being or could be used improperly for capital punishment, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment.
in writing. ‒ i reject the proposal for a regulation from the ep on the amendment of the regulation on trade in certain goods which can be used for capital punishment and other humiliating and inhuman treatment or punishment, because in this way and through its established policy, the eu can contribute to the abolition of the death penalty, the prevention of torture and at the same time help to achieve these objectives by combining a specific licensing scheme for trade in such goods. it is necessary to ensure controls on eu exports, including inventories of military equipment, dual-use items, firearms and instruments of torture. finally, i would stress the key role that national authorities should play in ensuring effective controls on these species and in establishing a system of regular reporting and review.
in writing. ‒ the the report prohibits the export of products which have no practical use other than for the execution or torture of human beings (e.g. electric chairs and automatic systems for intravenous injection of medicinal substances designed for the execution of human beings). it also introduces controls on the trade of products designed for other purposes but which could be used for torture or execution of human beings. as noted, it is important to properly combine eu legislative measures with administrative, judicial and external measures to prevent and prohibit the production, trade and use of instruments of torture and to effectively control objects that may be used for murderous and inhumane purposes. the absence of multilateral export control systems in the field of torture instruments inevitably makes this task more difficult. the adoption of this regulation is another step in our efforts to abolish the death penalty and torture everywhere in the world. i have voted in favour of the report for these reasons.
in writing. ‒ the the torture regulation is a tool that contributes to the protection of human rights by controlling the items and activities that are being or could be used improperly for capital punishment, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment. it is important to properly combine eu legislative measures with administrative, judicial and external measures to prevent and prohibit the production, trade and use of instruments of torture and to effectively control objects that may be used for murderous and inhumane purposes. the commission proposes to strengthen the controls on torture facilities by suggesting ways and the rapporteur in turn supports the approach of necessary and proportionate controls without unnecessary burden. for all these reasons, we voted in favour of the report.

in writing. ‒ the i support the report and stress that if we consider that the european union cannot be a closed club of countries concerned only with the development of the european union itself, but that, on the contrary, the european union has a responsibility and a role to play beyond its borders, then we must also consider that the agreement reached between the countries of the caribbean, the pacific and africa and the member states of the european union is the crowning achievement of a modern european union that responds to the calls of all humanity and is present wherever there is a problem. i believe that if one studies this agreement, in its sixteen years of existence, one will realize that, whatever problems there are, whatever difficulties and obstacles there may be, it is a very useful agreement which really does offer these countries and contributes both to their sustainable development and to their development and integration into the world economy.
in writing. ‒ i am voting in favour of the motion for a resolution on the future of acp-eu relations after 2020 because it strengthens the links between the peoples and acp countries and the eu countries and between their parliaments. in order to improve and to make cooperation effective, a new structure should be adopted which will maintain the universal features of the acp-eu acquis such as the attachment to human rights, to sound democracy and to human rights. finally, i support this resolution and stress that issues such as the fight against poverty, the fight against inequalities and the promotion of sustainable development should be the primary objective of acp-eu cooperation.
in writing. ‒ the the overall framework for eu relations with africa is the cotonou agreement, which was established in 2000 with the aim of eradicating poverty through the full integration of the acp countries into the world economy. however, the political and economic landscape of the acp countries and the european union has changed considerably since the cotonou agreement was signed. in order to improve the effectiveness of cooperation and to adapt it to new challenges, a new structure should be adopted which preserves the elements of the acp-eu acquis, such as the emphasis on human rights, good governance and democracy, the objective of the rule of law and the exchange of best practices in a common framework. i voted in favour of the report calling for the 2030 agenda and the sustainable development goals to be at the heart of the new agreement, as well as the establishment of an acp-eu peer monitoring, accountability and evaluation mechanism to regularly monitor the implementation of the sdgs in the member states.
in writing. ‒ the the acp-eu joint assembly has set as its principle the equal and fair negotiation between the acp and the eu for the continuation of its relations. unfortunately, the two large groups in parliament have put forward the commercial and economic interests of the big companies. they have supported, among other things, free trade agreements which will subjugate the peoples of the african, caribbean and pacific countries, as indeed all free trade agreements. at the same time, the calls of the etuc/bpa for integrated and sustainable development policies with the strengthening of the labour and democratic rights of all citizens were not heeded. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.

in writing. ‒ the i am voting against the report on the proposal for a council decision on the accession of peru to the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction because this convention is a vital instrument because it enables the states parties to work together to find a solution to the issue of international child abduction. it should be noted that the courts have jurisdiction but the law of the state where the child resides should apply. for the above reasons, and also because of the humanitarian nature of this convention, austria and romania should accept the accession of peru.
in writing. ‒ the the 1980 hague convention is a vital tool as it ensures the prompt return of children abducted by a parent to the same state of residence, as well as the protection of the rights of the parent to communicate with his or her child. as international parental child abduction is a global problem affecting thousands of children each year, peru's accession to the convention is a positive one. i voted in favour of the report because, as the rapporteur points out, many eu citizens are of peruvian origin, which means that, by acceding to the hague convention, the protection against abduction of children with links to the peruvian community will be ensured.
in writing. ‒ the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction is a vital instrument and establishes a system that enables states parties to cooperate in resolving situations of international child abduction. that is why i voted in favour of the report.

in writing. ‒ the i am voting against the report on the proposal for a council decision on the accession of kazakhstan to the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction because this convention is a vital instrument because it enables the states parties to work together to find a solution to the issue of international child abduction. i believe that, following the accession of kazakhstan to the convention, it is necessary to adopt a council decision authorizing the member states to accept this accession, since the hague convention system is more effective with the accession of more countries.
in writing. ‒ the the problem of child abduction is becoming more and more serious these days. the problem of this social phenomenon is difficult to tackle within the framework of each legal system. the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction is therefore a vital instrument. the commission, together with member states' experts in civil justice, has assessed the functioning of the justice system of kazakhstan and the readiness of the country to implement the convention and considers it desirable to bring the convention between the european union and kazakhstan into force. i therefore supported the report as the hague convention system becomes more efficient with the accession of more countries.

in writing. ‒ the i am voting against the report on the proposal for a council decision on the accession of the republic of korea to the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction, because this convention is a vital instrument since it enables the states parties to work together to find a solution to international child abduction. i therefore believe that, following south korea's accession to the convention, it is necessary for the council to adopt a decision authorising the member states to accept the accession of south korea, as this will allow the convention between south korea and the eu to enter into force in its entirety. in addition, the presence of the korean community in europe is strong, which makes the above even more important.
in writing. ‒ the preventing child abduction is an essential part of the eu's policy to promote children's rights. the european union is therefore working internationally to improve the implementation of the 1980 convention and is encouraging third countries to accede to it. the commission, together with member states' civil justice experts, has assessed the functioning of the justice system of south korea and the willingness of the country to implement the convention and considers it desirable to bring the convention between the european union and south korea into force. i therefore voted in favour of the report on the accession of korea to the 1980 hague convention because the hague convention system thus becomes more effective with the accession of more countries. the korean community has a strong presence in europe, which makes the acceptance of its accession all the more important.

in writing. ‒ i am voting in favour of the resolution on the eu's next steps on food security issues in the world because, taking into account the universal declaration of human rights and the international covenant on economic, social and cultural rights, we conclude that the right to food is an inalienable right since it is part of the right to an adequate standard of living, taking into account the right to food. moreover, the human right to food can only be realised when poverty and inequality are drastically reduced and also when equality is ensured. this can be achieved by creating rights-based social safety nets and ensuring the full participation of vulnerable and weaker groups. i therefore support the report.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report on achieving the objective of eradicating hunger and malnutrition by 2030, in particular by ensuring opportunities for increasing the productivity of small farms and by achieving sustainable and climate resilient agricultural and food systems. currently, 159 million children are sick and 50 million are affected because of the reckless waste of food. malnutrition has a huge impact on growth and health and reinforces the cycle of poverty. the level of investment remains insufficient. it is also important that the commission is called upon to set out a clear policy framework for increasing support to national social security networks, which have proven in several countries to be an important tool for increasing resilience and reducing malnutrition.
in writing. ‒ the the resolution addresses not only food security but also ensuring sustainable agriculture through improved governance and accountability. it also proposes systemic policies on food and nutrition, which take into account the gender dimension, sustainable agriculture, the use of and access to natural resources, public water, and public sanitation and hygiene. finally, the rights of vulnerable and marginalised groups are respected. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.

in writing. ‒ the i am voting in favour of the ep resolution on the european public prosecutor's office and eurojust because i believe it is very important for the eu and all its member states to detect and effectively combat fraud against the eu's financial interests so that the eu can protect the taxpayers of all the member states who contribute to the union's budget. in addition, eurojust has facilitated coordination and cooperation between national investigating and prosecuting authorities in cases involving most member states and has also contributed to the development of mutual trust and to bridging the gap between the various legal systems and traditions of the eu. it is also clear that eurojust has facilitated and improved crossborder criminal prosecution.
in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report on the european public prosecutor's office and the criminal justice cooperation agency because both the commission and the council are still in the process of internal consultations. the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office is subject to serious legal uncertainties and ambiguities which have not yet been resolved. all the reports on this subject which have been produced to date have bypassed the necessary careful study of the impact of the creation of such a body on national criminal proceedings and the concerns of many national parliaments. there is also particular concern whether this proposed institution, in the form and with the powers envisaged, is the most appropriate option for dealing with the problem of fraud affecting the eu's financial interests, while respecting the principle of subsidiarity. but we also disagree on the substance of the transfer of more and more powers and responsibilities to the eu, powers which are currently exclusively held by the member states.

in writing. ‒ the i am voting against the motion for a resolution on the need for a european industrial policy in the light of the recent caterpillar and alstom cases because european industry has played an important role in the history of european integration and is a pillar of new values in europe. it should also be borne in mind that the european rail industry is the cornerstone and backbone of european industrialisation and at the same time employs 400,000 people across the eu. on the other hand, only through an ambitious innovation policy that promotes the production of innovative products will the eu be allowed to be self-sufficient in a competitive global environment. given that in the case of alstom and caterpillar the expertise of the workers' representatives has not been taken into account and that in many cases the closure of factories often entails irreversible losses in technologies, industrial know-how and the skills of industrial workers, i believe that the commission should put forward a proposal for a revision of the member states' legislation on collective redundancies.
in writing. ‒ the the joint resolution of parliament was not at all satisfactory. we on the left see the need for reindustrialisation but not in the light of supporting the multinationals at all costs. despite billions in profits, these two multinationals decided to close their factories, leaving thousands of workers on the street and affecting dozens of other businesses. the resolution condemns in the most mild terms this action by the multinationals. in our view, it should require public investment both in creating new industrial sectors and in safe and decent jobs, and in supporting workers. we cannot support a resolution which requires the state to be a crutch for profitable multinationals.

in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the resolution on international financial reporting standards, as ifrs 9 'financial instruments' is one of the most important international accounting standards board (iasb) responses to the financial crisis and implementation efforts are already underway. in addition, ifrs 9 provides an improved model over ias 39. that resolution makes a significant reference to the commission in order to ensure that ifrs 9 serves the long-term investment strategy of the eu and limits pro-cyclicality and incentives to take excessive risks. it is also important that the commission, in cooperation with the european supervisory authorities, the ecb, the european systemic risk board and efrag, monitors the implementation of ifrs 9 in the eu in order to prepare an ex post impact assessment, which is very important for the smooth implementation of ifrs 9.
in writing. ‒ the the resolution actually expresses parliament's support for the g20 decisions and stresses that the proposed countercyclical measures are in the right direction. some concerns are expressed about the complexity of the new standards, but there is a reference to an 'immediate response to the crisis'. accounting standards are thought to have played a role in the profitability of banks and investment groups before the crisis. however, not only does it not call for the immediate consolidation of banking standards or the regulation of the operations of investment groups, but it also fits into the logic of 'impact assessment'. it is a very general resolution on a very technocratic subject. the real cause of the crisis-banks/investment groups relationship, which is the absolute freedom given to these institutions to speculate, is not addressed. that is why we have chosen to abstain.

in writing. ‒ i vote against the report on the application of the regulation on materials in contact with food, because when they come into contact with food, and depending on their composition and properties, different materials behave differently and can transfer their constituents to food. member states often adopt specific measures at national level, but these may differ from those of other member states, resulting in different standards for product safety. for these reasons, eu action is necessary to address both the lack of concrete measures and the gaps in risk assessment, compliance and control.
in writing. ‒ the food, before its final consumption, comes into contact with many materials and objects during the various stages of its production, processing, storage, preparation and disposal. these materials must be sufficiently inert so that their constituents do not adversely affect the health of consumers or the quality of food. as food is one of the most important routes of human exposure to chemicals, food contact materials are subject to legally binding rules at eu level. i therefore voted in favour of the report on the application of the regulation on materials in contact with foodstuffs because it seeks to ensure a high level of consumer protection while ensuring the effective functioning of the internal market in relation to products in contact with foodstuffs.
in writing. ‒ the report provides for the examination by eu committees of food contact materials imported from third countries, although it does so without strong and clear language. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them.

in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report because it contains correct assessments of the transposition and application of eu law. as it states, environment, health, transport, internal market and services remain the policy areas in which most infringement cases remained pending in 2014. given that parliament still has limited access to information on the eu pilot procedure as well as to pending cases, it is not easy to assess to what extent the reduction in formal infringement procedures actually reflects member states' compliance with union law rather than compromise solutions between the commission and member states that are in breach of union law. the report therefore shows that the enforcement of eu law is still characterised by a lack of transparency.

in writing. ‒ the china is among those countries whose nationals are required to have a visa when travelling in the schengen area. the same applies to eu citizens wishing to enter china. the agreement provides for visa-free travel for eu citizens holding an eu diplomatic passport or eu transit authorisation and for chinese citizens when travelling to the territory of the other party for a maximum period of 90 days in any 180-day period. the abolition of visas for holders of diplomatic passports is therefore a positive step towards the introduction of full visa exemption for holders of common passports. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.

in writing. ‒ the countries in budgetary difficulties have difficulty in allocating sufficient funds to co-finance regional policy projects. articles 24 and 120 (3) of the common provisions regulation aim to provide assistance to member states in budgetary difficulties and to those receiving financial assistance. the modification of the period of time during which a country receiving financial assistance will be eligible for the additional payments and the fact that a member state continues to be eligible until 30 june of the year following the calendar year in which it ceases to receive financial assistance through a macroeconomic adjustment programme are steps in the right direction. in cyprus, which would normally receive 50% of the co-financing for erdf and esf programmes, an 85% co-financing rate is granted because it is facing economic difficulties and reduced investment. the increased co-financing will have a positive impact on the cypriot economy, as it will reduce the amounts allocated to national co-financing and support fiscal consolidation and investment efforts. i therefore voted in favour of this report.

in writing. ‒ we reject the report and stress that organised crime is a global threat and as such requires a joint and coordinated response by the eu and its member states.
in writing. ‒ the this report is the necessary follow-up to the resolution adopted in october 2013 on organised crime, corruption and money laundering. it contains both positive and negative elements. some of the good recommendations included in the report are the fact that it asks the eu to become a member of greco and the commission to publish its report on the fight against corruption and to propose appropriate legislation. it points to the need for legislation that will strengthen the rights of suspects and guarantees of a fair trial, and calls on the commission to submit a legislative proposal for the protection of witnesses and whistleblowers before the end of 2017. however, three key issues raised in the report tend to be of concern. firstly, the permanent loss of the right to be elected as a member of the european parliament after one conviction for a criminal offence; secondly, references to the need to place greater emphasis on membership of a criminal organisation than on crime; and thirdly, references to the european public prosecutor's office. for these reasons, we abstained.
in writing. ‒ the organised crime is a global threat and requires a joint and coordinated response by the eu and its member states. moreover, organised crime, corruption and money laundering seriously threaten both the eu economy, significantly reducing tax revenues for member states and the eu as a whole, and democratic control of eu-funded public works. i voted in favour of the report which focuses on specific areas where priority action is needed. for example, measures are required to ensure the correct transposition of existing rules, to monitor their application and to assess their effectiveness. the report also calls for the creation of a specialised europol unit to combat organised crime and corruption, a stricter legislative framework and more effective judicial and police cooperation at eu level. the importance of preventing the penetration of organised crime and corruption into the legal economy and the role of the european public prosecutor's office in fighting corruption in the eu are also underlined. finally, confiscating the assets of criminal groups in the eu and facilitating their reuse for social purposes would act as a deterrent for criminals.
in writing. ‒ the the report contains both positive and negative elements. some of the good recommendations included in the report are that the eu should become a member of greco and that the commission should publish its report on the fight against corruption and propose appropriate legislation. it also states the need for legislation that will strengthen the rights of suspects as well as guarantees for a fair trial, and requires the commission to submit a legislative proposal for the protection of witnesses and whistleblowers before the end of 2017. however, two key issues raised in the report are of particular concern. firstly, references to the need to place more emphasis on participation in a criminal organisation than on the crime itself, and also references to the establishment of a european public prosecutor's office, which would be the central element in the fight against corruption, which implies the transfer of more and more powers and responsibilities that member states currently have to the eu. for these reasons, we abstained.

in writing. ‒ the economic activities in europe and the quality of life of our citizens depend to a large extent on high-quality transport links. transport networks well connected to serve passenger and freight traffic at local, regional, national and cross-border level and easy access to them are a driving force for strengthening economic, social and geographical cohesion within and between member states in the eu. despite this progress, serious gaps in the accessibility and connectivity of transport infrastructure across the central and eastern eu remain. these gaps should be closed in order to maintain economic growth and employment opportunities for citizens and businesses to the greatest extent possible. the (eastern) border regions are in particular urgent need of such intervention, otherwise they will continue to lose their populations. in addition, the maintenance of sustainable transport links should be seen as an increasingly important factor in strengthening the stability and security of the eu's borders in eastern europe and the western balkans. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and underline that the european union has integrated democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights into its basic principles and objectives set out in the first articles of the treaties, as well as in the eu accession criteria.
in writing. ‒ the the report provides for the establishment of an eu mechanism to monitor member states in relation to democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. although the mechanism is primarily aimed at preventing violations and non-compliance with the principles of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights, it explicitly provides that the means will be provided to impose corrective measures and sanctions against member states. the report's main weakness is that the supervision of the action of the eu institutions is neither sufficiently rigorous nor clearly defined, in relation to the corresponding structure-procedure proposed for the member states. in addition to the three institutions, the other eu institutions and bodies escape supervision and control. there is no provision for the court of justice of the eu to intervene in decisions taken on the basis of article 7 teu, and there are no clear references to social rights as an integral part of fundamental rights. the whole question of creating such a mechanism is approached through the more general position on the issue of giving further powers to the eu and reducing the sovereignty of the member states. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.
in writing. ‒ i am voting against this resolution because it is imperative to establish a framework that will give the eu the opportunity to address violations and threats against democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. in addition, additional non-legislative measures should be taken to ensure an integrated approach to respecting the principles on which the eu is based.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report which proposes the creation of a european binding mechanism which would provide for the annual reporting on the state of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights in all eu member states. the mechanism will ensure that all eu member states respect the values enshrined in the european treaties and will set clear, documented and non-political criteria for assessing countries' track records in the areas of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. the mechanism aims to integrate existing tools into a single tool and ensure their effective use. it also wants to bridge the gap between the monitoring of the respect for these values by the candidate countries on the one hand and the lack of effective means of monitoring the respect for the same values by those who are already eu member states on the other. we don't want to be left with a theory. the recent events in hungary and poland have shown that the eu must have effective tools at its disposal to counter threats to the eu's fundamental values, threats that undermine democracy in our countries.
in writing. ‒ the the report provides for the establishment of an eu mechanism to monitor member states in relation to democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. although the mechanism is primarily aimed at preventing infringements, it explicitly provides that the means will be provided to impose corrective measures and sanctions against member states. a key weakness of the report is that the supervision of the action of the eu institutions is neither sufficiently rigorous nor clearly defined, even in relation to the corresponding structure-procedure proposed for the member states. nor is there any provision for the court of justice of the eu to intervene in decisions taken on the basis of article 7 teu. nor are there clear references to social rights as an integral part of fundamental rights, recognised by international and european conventions and which are usually absent from the approach of the eu institutions to fundamental rights. finally, the whole issue of creating an eu mechanism for state supervision is part of the broader effort to give further powers to the eu and to reduce the sovereignty of the member states. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training. we therefore voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ the commission should set up a binding eu mechanism to monitor and report annually on cases of breaches of human rights, democracy, rule of law and fundamental rights in eu member states; a mechanism that should include objective benchmarks and lay down a gradual approach to remedying breaches. our core values are currently not protected by instruments that are strong enough to make sure that these values are upheld throughout the entire european union. this new eu mechanism should ensure that all eu member states respect the values enshrined in the eu treaties and set clear, evidence-based and non-political criteria for assessing their records on democracy, rule of law and fundamental rights in a systematic way and on an equal footing

in writing. ‒ the high consumption of trans fats is a risk factor, in particular for the development of coronary heart disease, a disease which is responsible for around 660,000 deaths per year in the eu. these fatty acids are used more in cheaper foods, which means that people with low incomes are more exposed to foods high in trans-fatty acids. this, of course, exacerbates health inequalities. i voted against this resolution, which calls for mandatory limits on the use of industrially produced, partially hydrogenated vegetable oils (trans-fatty acids) which increase the risk of cardiovascular disease, infertility, alzheimer's, diabetes and obesity. the labelling of these products is important but not sufficient. it must be accompanied by the imposition of mandatory limits.
in writing. ‒ the the report stresses that mandatory limits should be set on the use of industrially produced trans-fatty acids, which can increase the risk of cardiovascular disease, infertility, alzheimer's, diabetes and obesity. it is also rightly pointed out that the existing measures on product labelling are not sufficient, with the result that consumers are not aware of the existence of trans-fatty acids. the report therefore calls on the commission to propose, as soon as possible, a maximum permitted limit for the use of trans-fatty acids in all foodstuffs. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community.

in writing. ‒ the the resolution calls on turkey to immediately end the arrests, threats, violations and restrictions it imposes on journalists and their families. according to the european federation of journalists and the turkish journalists organization, since july 15, more than 99 journalists/intellectuals have been arrested without charge. if you add the media workers who have been arrested, the number comes to 130. the resolution also calls on turkey to relax the state of emergency, which, in conjunction with the existing anti-terrorism law, destroys any notion of the rule of law. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them.
in writing. ‒ the the failed coup in july left 241 dead and thousands injured, with 90 journalists imprisoned, over 2,500 jobless and arrest warrants issued against hundreds of media workers in the country. in late september, the turkish government shut down some 20 tv and radio channels accusing them of "spreading terrorist propaganda" and extended by 90 days the state of emergency declared after the coup attempt (from october 19). can dündar, former editor-in-chief of cumhuriyet, turkey's main opposition daily newspaper, was arrested in november 2015 after his newspaper published reports showing turkish intelligence officials smuggling weapons to syrian rebels. he was later sentenced to five years and ten months in prison for "revealing state secrets". freedom of speech is a fundamental right which must be accepted and respected by all. we must safeguard this right so that objective and impartial information can continue to be available.
in writing. ‒ the the situation of press freedom in turkey has deteriorated over the past decade and especially after the failed coup, as the turkish government abuses the state of emergency it declared to severely restrict freedom of speech and the media. in total, about 100 journalists and writers were arrested. most of them haven't even been charged. 104 media outlets have been closed, resulting in over 2,300 journalists losing their jobs, press accreditation has been revoked for at least 330 journalists, and arrest warrants have been issued against hundreds of media workers in the country. all this is mentioned in the european parliament's resolution. the resolution also stresses that the failed coup cannot be used as a pretext to silence the opposition and journalists and calls on the turkish authorities to release immediately the journalists and media workers detained without charge. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the context of the directive. the european union is also calling on turkey to stop violating freedom of expression. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.

in writing. ‒ the the purpose of the directive is to reduce national emissions of certain air pollutants in order to address the extremely significant health risks and environmental impacts, in particular of air pollution in the union. however, the report shows serious shortcomings in particular with regard to the removal of the methane and mercury emission ceilings and the absence of binding interim (2025) targets for reducing emissions (there is only a reference to indicative targets). we therefore voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report which calls for reducing the national emission ceilings for key pollutants by 2030, including nitrogen oxides, particulate matter and sulphur dioxide. poor air quality is responsible for over 400,000 premature deaths each year and the economic cost to health is estimated at between €300-900 billion each year. some 90% of europeans living in cities are exposed to air pollution levels considered harmful to human health, and many vulnerable socio-demographic groups are particularly affected. the new agreement to reduce national emission limits will cut the number of premature deaths from toxic air by almost half by 2030, forcing governments across europe to act and ensure, for the first time, that all polluting sectors, including agriculture, take responsibility for what they emit. the member states must now act to fully implement the directive and, moreover, to put into practice their ambition to combat air pollution.
in writing. ‒ the the case of air quality is a cross-border health and environmental issue. this report concerns the commission proposal to revise the existing ets directive by setting new emission ceilings for sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides, ammonia and volatile organic compounds other than methane for 2020 and 2030. the report is not without its weaknesses. the text is not ambitious in terms of emission limits, and there is flexibility in the application of the directive by the member states, which makes the application of the limits very difficult. in addition, there are no binding interim targets for 2025. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and stress that a resilient and well-capitalised banking system is a prerequisite for maintaining financial stability.
in writing. ‒ the we voted against the report because our main concern was that european banks should not be at a disadvantage compared to their counterparts in the usa. the concern of the financial sector in the european union is that the proposals could lead to significantly higher capital requirements especially for banks using internal models. the report is based on the need to create a level playing field and proportionality, especially for smaller and less complex banks. while we agree with some of these concerns (in relation to smaller and less risky banks), we fundamentally disagree with the fact that the banking industry should not be overburdened. we believe that internal models in banks were a major cause of the financial crisis, as they were often used to hide significant risks on banks' balance sheets.

in writing. ‒ we reject the report and note that the european security environment has deteriorated significantly, becoming more fluid, more complex, more dangerous and less predictable.
in writing. ‒ the report calls for an increase in the military component of the csdp, more investment in defence and defence research. we are opposed to the report because it supports deep cooperation with nato, calls for a strong defence industry and welcomes the european action plan, which benefits the defence and security industries of the european union. we call for radical (including chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear) disarmament at european union level and at world level, no military funding from the european union budget, strict separation of the european union from nato and a non-military european union. a european union that will promote peace and solve problems through dialogue and not through the use of arms.
in writing. ‒ the the implementation of the common security and defence policy provides greater support to member states fighting the islamic state. i voted in favour of the report calling on the council to set up a fund to provide emergency funding for the initial phase of military operations and calling on the eu to support in a coherent way the member states participating in the coalition against the self-proclaimed islamic state, through the creation of a common security and defence policy operation in iraq focused on training. security has become a major problem for eu citizens. we call for a full and substantial revision of the common security and defence policy, which can no longer be merely an instrument for crisis management outside the union, because it must ensure a genuine european common security and defence policy, bridging internal and external security, but also become an eu tool for managing international crises at all stages of the conflict cycle. effective implementation of recent decisions on the development of cepol is vital to protect citizens and promote eu values and interests, in full respect of the treaties, international law and human rights.
in writing. ‒ we support the report and call on the relevant eu institutions and authorities to monitor closely the sources of funding for anti-eu propaganda.
in writing. ‒ the the report highlights two threats in terms of propaganda: a state entity, namely russia, and terrorist groups such as daesh. this indirect attempt to link the two is not at all accidental, since it attempts to present russia's role in eastern europe similar to that of daesh in the middle east. this is unacceptable to us. the report also sets out a number of synergies that the european union and the member states should pursue in cooperation with nato to address these situations. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. ‒ the we stress that the fight against propaganda cannot be done with more propaganda but with more positive messages and awareness and information campaigns. the draft resolution stresses that the european union is under increasing systematic pressure to counter disinformation and propaganda campaigns by various countries and non-state actors using a wide range of tools, such as fake news agencies and online 'trolls', aimed at undermining democratic values and dividing europe. but i'm not equating russia with the islamic state. there has to be a clear separation and we must not equate russia with the islamic state. it's irresponsible to put a state like russia on the same level of threat as the islamic state. russia must be recognised as a key partner of the european union and a key global player, both in external security and in the common fight against the islamic state.
in writing. ‒ the report attacks journalism and calls on journalists to use nato tools, such as the strategic communications centre, to 'fight' the russian media. it also seeks to equate russia with terrorist organizations. finally, the report identifies a number of synergies that the european union and the member states should pursue in cooperation with nato to address 'dangerous situations'. we have therefore voted against the report.

in writing. ‒ we voted against the report because it is moving in the right direction by calling on the european union to recognise the needs of deaf and hard of hearing people and to take all the necessary measures to provide them with all the necessary professional interpretation services.
in writing. ‒ the there are about one million deaf sign language users in the eu and 51 million hard of hearing citizens, many of whom are also sign language users. the resolution, which i voted in favour of, stresses the need for official recognition of national and regional sign languages in the member states and in the eu institutions, the strengthening of the role of professional sign language interpreters, the provision of high-quality sign language interpretation services in order to strengthen the fight against discrimination on the grounds of disability. the resolution also stresses the need to ensure accessibility, i.e. that deaf, hard of hearing and hard of hearing citizens have access to the same information and communication with their fellow citizens through sign language interpretation, subtitling, speech recognition and/or alternative forms of communication. as full citizens, all persons with disabilities have equal rights and are entitled to respect for their dignity, equal treatment, independent living, autonomy and full participation in society.
in writing. ‒ the the report is moving in the right direction by calling on the european union to recognise the needs of deaf and hard of hearing people and to take all the necessary measures to provide them with all the necessary professional interpretation services. it also called for the implementation of holistic policies in this area. our group tried to strengthen the report by introducing amendments on specific policies in education systems, but without finding support from other political groups. we voted against the report.

in writing. ‒ the the proposal aims to improve the governance and transparency of institutions providing occupational pension benefits in europe, to promote cross-border activity and to facilitate long-term investment. a delicate balance is also sought between strengthening national systems, respecting national discretion and encouraging the development of cross-border systems. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. the proposal, which i voted for, reinforces the reference to national social and labour provisions, with a focus on the protection of insured persons, while introducing the concept of a duty of care, in order to avoid a regulatory race to the bottom through cross-border schemes.
in writing. ‒ this is a directive that fully promotes the privatisation of pension systems. the european parliament's report stresses, however, that the sovereign right of the member states to determine the provisions of their pension funds must not be interfered with. it is a very small improvement on the directive, since the principle of the directive remains unchanged. that is why we abstained.

in writing. ‒ the four months after the failed coup attempt in turkey, the deterioration of the democratic situation in the country is causing a stir in eu-turkey relations. accession negotiations with turkey were opened in october 2005. of the 35 chapters required to be completed to proceed with the accession process, only 16 have been opened and of these only one has been closed, that for science and research. the accession talks with turkey must be suspended temporarily. on the initiative of the socialist and democratic group, of which i am a member, the european parliament stresses in this resolution that there is no alternative solution as long as the turkish authorities continue to neglect and undermine democracy and the rule of law. the dialogue with ankara should be maintained, but erdogan must understand that in turkey, as a candidate country, the rule of law, democracy, freedom of the press and minority rights must be unshakable pillars. the eu must send a strong political message by freezing accession talks until the turkish government returns to the path of respect for the rule of law and human rights.

in writing. ‒ we voted against the report on the european ombudsman, as we recognise the efforts that have been made/are being made to enhance transparency on such serious issues as ttip, the european citizens' initiative, fundamental rights in the context of the european union's cohesion policy, the frontex complaint and the 'revolving doors' inquiry, an issue that has come back to the fore after barroso's appointment to goldman sachs.
in writing. ‒ the the report welcomes the work of the european ombudsman and highlights the main areas of activity for the year 2015. the role of the european ombudsman's office is to investigate complaints from citizens about maladministration by the eu institutions or other eu bodies, or it can conduct inquiries on its own initiative, with the aim of making the eu institutions more open and citizen-friendly. the year was particularly marked by the theme of transparency (ttip negotiations, ecb, eu decision-making, whistleblower and conflict of interest). the european ombudsman also investigated the respect for fundamental rights, in particular with regard to the role of frontex, as well as the spending of money by member states on projects funded by eu funds, for example on projects for people with disabilities.
in writing. ‒ the the report endorses the activity report submitted in 2015 and congratulates it. welcomes the efforts to consolidate transparency in the various institutions and bodies of the european union and underlines the 8 strategic inquiries launched on important issues such as transparency in ttip negotiations, disclosure of documents, the european citizens' initiative, fundamental rights in the context of eu cohesion policy and the complaint against frontex. it also focuses on the opening up of the 6 strategic initiatives, including the ecb's transparency, the activities of former commissioners, and the asylum, migration, and integration fund. the report generally takes a critical approach to a number of important issues. that is why we voted in favour of it.
in writing. ‒ the vat generated almost €1 trillion in revenue in 2014 and is a major source of revenue in the eu. it represents about 7% of the union's gdp and provides part of the eu's own resources. from the outset, the current vat system was prone to fraud and is now largely outdated. despite numerous initiatives to correct this, the problem remains. i voted in favour of this report which makes proposals to improve the vat collection system, eliminate vat fraud and reduce red tape, aim to further simplify business for smes and, most importantly, underlines the need for harmonisation at eu level in tax policy.
in writing. ‒ while simplification of the vat system is needed to combat fraud, it cannot be done for more profiteering by multinationals. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information it receives. the report does not touch on these issues at all. we voted against the report.

in writing. ‒ the the illegal wildlife trade has developed into a multi-billion euro criminal industry controlled by organised criminal groups. the low risk of detection and high profits attract gangs, which exploit illegal wildlife trapping and transport to finance their criminal activities. the action plan sets out priorities for the period 2016-2020 that will strengthen the european union's role in combating wildlife trafficking globally: prevention, law enforcement, cooperation, and recognition of the european union as a destination, source and transit market for wildlife products. that is why we voted in favour of this report.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of this action plan because the european union is an important player in the fight against wildlife crime, because it is a major destination for illegal wildlife products, as well as a transit point and often a source for illegal wildlife trade, especially between african, asian and latin american countries, but also within the european union itself. the action plan is part of the european union's response to the un 2030 agenda for sustainable development. the first priority of the action plan is to prevent wildlife trafficking and to tackle its root causes. the second priority is to ensure the effective implementation and enforcement of existing rules to combat organised wildlife crime. the third priority is to strengthen global cooperation among countries of origin, consumption and transit to combat wildlife trafficking. finally, the member states of the european union have a shared responsibility to tackle this problem and prevent the rapid decline of some of the rarest wild species in the world.

in writing. ‒ the the report criticises anti-competitive practices, which can lead to serious distortions, such as social dumping. small transport enterprises are therefore an integral part of mobility in europe. they also have a wealth of talents that need a supportive environment to express themselves. the most favourable regulatory, social and tax conditions are needed, because only then will small businesses be able to turn the many challenges they face into opportunities. the information and communication needs of the transport sector are so important that they are turning the sector into a fertile ground for the digital economy and, above all, for mediation platforms. the new technologies, which have become accessible to small businesses, are significantly modernising the mobility economy. the first challenge for small businesses is to be able to take advantage of this digital transition. secondly, we are in the best possible situation to develop sustainable solutions which will enable us, despite the limited capacity of transport infrastructure, to meet the growing needs of mobility in an environmentally friendly way. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of this report.

in writing. ‒ the the competent authorities in the member states do not have a legal basis to apply the provisions relating to lodging the entry summary declaration, notification of the arrival of a seagoing ship or aircraft, the transport and presentation of goods to customs on unloading or transhipment, the waiting for the granting of a permit before unloading or transhipment of goods and the provisions on temporary storage. there is a lack of a clear legal basis requiring the presentation of goods to customs authorities. the latter find that their effective customs supervision is impeded in that they are unable to properly enforce import duties and other charges, to properly apply non-tax measures such as health checks, and to identify risks relating to goods arriving at their ports or airports. the proposal to amend article 136 is intended to fill this gap and to ensure equal treatment of goods.

in writing. ‒ the the european union solidarity fund (eusf) was set up to respond to major natural disasters and to show european solidarity in the affected areas of europe. the fund was set up following the severe floods that hit central europe in the summer of 2002. since then, it has been used on 72 occasions to deal with disasters such as floods, forest fires, earthquakes, storms, and droughts. to date, over €3.8 billion in aid has been provided to 24 different european countries. i voted in favour of the report, which emphasises a number of recommendations for possible improvements in the management of the fund in areas such as speed of response, prevention and complementarity, transparency and cooperation between the different levels of decision-making.
in writing. ‒ the i voted in favour of the report on the eu-us agreement on the protection of personal data exchanged by judicial and police authorities. the aim of the agreement is to ensure high and binding standards of data protection for data exchanged by the police and law enforcement authorities. the agreement covers all personal data, such as names, addresses or criminal records, exchanged between the eu and the us for the purposes of preventing, detecting, investigating and prosecuting criminal offences, including terrorism. the agreement guarantees eu and us citizens: the right to administrative and judicial redress; the right to be informed of a breach of their data security; and the right to have their personal data corrected if inaccurate. so there will be high, binding standards and enshrined rights for citizens in both the eu and the us when data is exchanged between police and law enforcement agencies.
in writing. ‒ having examined all other budgetary options for addressing unforeseen circumstances within the 2016 commitment ceilings for heading 3 of the multiannual financial framework and having mobilised the flexibility mechanism for the entire amount of eur 1530 million available for 2016, it is necessary to mobilise the contingency margin to cover the needs arising from the crisis in the areas of migration, refugees and security by increasing the commitment appropriations of the general budget of the european union for the financial year 2016 above the commitment ceiling for heading 3.
in writing. ‒ the the main update of draft amending budget (dab) 4/2016 is the reduction of the amount of payment appropriations by eur 7,3 billion. this money is returned to the member states. draft amending budget no 4/2016 also includes, inter alia, the increase of eur 240,1 million in security and citizenship commitment appropriations and the acceleration of staffing for the new european border and coast guard regulation.
in writing. ‒ the the commission proposes to mobilise the european union solidarity fund (eusf) to provide financial assistance in response to the disaster in germany (floods in the region of lower bavaria, and in particular the district of rottal-inn) in may and june 2016. the series of extremely intense and short-lived/instantaneous floods caused damage to public and private infrastructure, private housing and agriculture. seven people were killed and more than 47,000 people in lower bavaria were injured, while more than 2,000 people were unable to return to their homes two months after the floods. the town of simbach am inn was almost completely destroyed and was without water for three weeks. significant damage to businesses and farms was also reported, as well as severe damage to basic infrastructure, the water network, the sewerage services and the electricity network.
in writing. ‒ having regard to draft amending budget no 6/2016 covering the proposal to mobilise the european union solidarity fund, in relation to the floods in germany in may and june 2016, taking into account that the commission therefore proposes to amend the 2016 budget and to increase the appropriations for budget article 13 06 01 'assistance to member states in the event of a major natural disaster with serious consequences for living conditions, the natural environment or the economy' by eur 31 475 125 in both commitment and payment appropriations, and that the european union solidarity fund is a specific instrument as defined in the mff regulation and that the corresponding appropriations should be entered in the budget above and beyond the ceilings, voted in favour of that proposal.
in writing. ‒ the the european union solidarity fund (eusf) was established to enable the union to respond to emergencies caused by major natural disasters. this proposal for a decision of the european parliament and of the council aims to enter in the general budget of the union for 2017 an amount of eur 50 million in commitment and payment appropriations for the provision of advances in a timely and effective manner in the event of a disaster in the coming year. this is a direct result of the new provision in the 2014 amending regulation, which aims to speed up the disbursement of aid to affected countries by granting an advance before a final decision on the granting of aid is taken, thereby enhancing the effectiveness of the fund. the eusr is not just another form of eu financial support, but also helps to send a clear political message of solidarity and provide the necessary assistance to the affected citizens. the possibility of rapid reaction, including by granting an advance, will help the union to achieve these objectives.
in writing. ‒ the the devastating earthquake that struck central italy in august this year has left a tragic legacy: human losses, thousands of displaced people, serious damage to local and regional infrastructure. all this is dealing a heavy blow to the historical and cultural heritage and economic activities. in addition, the italian national, regional and local public authorities are under great and intense financial pressure. it is obvious that in such emergencies an expression of solidarity is invaluable. i voted in favour of the resolution calling on the commission and the member states to further simplify the procedures for activating the european union civil protection mechanism so that it can be quickly and effectively available immediately after a disaster. this should be a priority given the high seismicity of south-east europe. it also stresses the importance of creating synergies between all available instruments, including the european structural and investment funds, to ensure that resources are actually used for reconstruction activities and all other necessary interventions, in full cooperation with the italian or, in the future, national and regional authorities.

in writing. ‒ the the report focuses on the importance of updating existing civil liability systems in the member states to ensure that the 'polluter pays' principle is applied not only for environmental damage but also for traditional damage (personal injury/material damage and consequent economic loss). incidents in european waters must not adversely affect the future of offshore oil and gas activities in that state or in the eu as a whole.

in writing. i am in favour of the adoption of a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing specific conditions for fishing for deep-sea stocks in the north-east atlantic and provisions for fishing in international waters of the north-east atlantic and repealing the regulation. parliament needs to introduce new and stricter transparency rules, including obligations to publish information on european vessels targeting deep-sea species and reporting on all fisheries in the ecosystem.
in writing. ‒ the deep-sea fishing in the north-east atlantic accounts for about 1% of landings from the north-east atlantic. the economy of many fishing communities depends to some extent on the fishing of deep-sea species. deep-sea stocks are fish stocks caught in waters other than the main shelf fisheries. i voted in favour of the report, which raises, among other things, the issue of sustainable management of stocks and the issue of preventing significant adverse impacts on vulnerable marine ecosystems. the european union will freeze its fishing footprint by specifying in which areas deep-sea fishing will be allowed. the area includes the area where directed fishing took place between 2009 and 2011 in eu waters of the north-east atlantic. in addition, a fishing limit of 800 metres was agreed. this new measure aims to protect more effectively the fragile deep-sea marine ecosystems. similarly, it was agreed that vessels caught fishing in vulnerable marine ecosystems at a depth of more than 400 metres should cease their operations and move at least five nautical miles from the fishing area concerned.

in writing. ‒ the the report on the human rights situation in the eu is produced annually and aims to present the situation in the eu on the basis of human rights and in addition to make recommendations for improving the existing situation. in addition to some positive elements contained in the report, a number of key weaknesses are presented. the report makes no mention of the negative consequences of the crisis and austerity measures that prevent access to fundamental rights, social rights and access to the most basic human needs such as education and health care. in addition, there is no mention of the need for the right to protection against poverty and social exclusion. the absence of reference to social rights as an integral part of fundamental rights is significant, as they are recognised by international and european conventions, yet they are generally absent from the approach of the eu institutions to fundamental rights. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them.

in writing. ‒ the human dignity, freedom, democracy, equality, the rule of law, and respect for human rights, including the rights of persons belonging to minorities, are the values on which the eu is founded. however, racism, discrimination, extremism and intolerance are a major challenge for the european union today. the complex crises and widespread violations and abuses of human rights and fundamental freedoms today require increasingly determined efforts by the eu. i voted in favour of the report, which sets out the main issues to be highlighted, which are, inter alia, the fight against human trafficking and smuggling networks, the fight against discrimination and prejudice against religion or belief, age, disability, sexual orientation or gender identity, the fight against violence against women, freedom of the media, communication and information, addressing violations of the fundamental rights of migrants and refugees at the eu's external borders and social inclusion and integration into the host society of those who are victims of international crime, combating child poverty.
in writing. ‒ the the report contains both positive and negative reports. some of the positive reports relate to refugees and the current asylum framework, the massive violation of prisoners' rights, the rise of racism and xenophobia. however, the report makes no mention of the negative consequences of the crisis and austerity measures that prevent access to fundamental rights, as well as access to the most basic human needs such as education and health care. in addition, there is no mention of the need for the right to protection against poverty and social exclusion. the negative aspects of the report may also include references to the immediate establishment of the european public prosecutor's office with specific and exclusive powers and the proposal to give the commission the power to impose sanctions on member states. we have therefore voted against the report.

in writing. ‒ the although article 167 tfeu expressly excludes harmonisation in the field of culture, stating that the eu's competence is only to encourage cooperation between states and to complement their action to improve knowledge and dissemination of culture and to protect cultural heritage, this report nevertheless seeks to establish a concrete framework setting out the conditions for the management of culture as another profitable industry. even the individual positive references in the report to the need to increase funding in the cultural and creative sectors, support smes and tackle gender-related discrimination in the labour market are overridden by the ultimate goal of serving market needs and harnessing the full potential of the cultural and creative sectors as another tool to enhance competitiveness. we therefore voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ the i am voting against the eu's coherent policy on the cultural and creative industries because i believe that the cultural and creative industries (ccis) have a dual value: first, they play a key role in preserving and promoting europe's cultural and linguistic diversity, reinforcing a sense of european and regional identity, contributing to the transmission of knowledge and values, while safeguarding europe's tangible and intangible heritage for present and future generations. at the same time, they are a tool for social cohesion, through their significant contribution to the eu economy in terms of employment, investment, growth and innovation, and make a significant contribution to europe's global competitiveness. finally, it is becoming strategically important to support innovative and research-oriented ict as they are able to maintain linguistic and cultural diversity, pluralism and the provision of innovative and high-quality services, especially in the context of a converging and globalised market.
in writing. ‒ the cultural and creative industries (ccis) are those industries based on cultural values, individual creativity, skills and talent, with the potential to create wealth and jobs through the production of value from intellectual property. the cultural and creative sector is considered to be a major and growing part of the global economy. i voted in favour of the report, which focuses on a number of measures to make full use of the potential of the cfp. to realise the full potential of the sector, coordinated action is needed at national, regional and local level, but also at eu level. as regards financing, the emphasis is on new forms of financing such as micro-credit or crowdfunding and the role of the commission and the member states in promoting and improving the regulatory framework. finally, as the report stresses, the eu could take a leading role in promoting the significant positive impact of the creative sectors across europe and also promote policies to safeguard europe's most unique asset: its culture.
in writing. ‒ the although article 167 tfeu expressly excludes harmonisation in the field of culture, this report seeks to establish a specific framework setting out the conditions for managing culture as yet another profitable industry, which, by using specific indicators measuring economic impact, can be used to enhance the union's competitiveness. even the individual positive references in the report to the need to increase funding in the cultural and creative sectors, to support smes and to tackle discrimination in the labour market, are overridden by the ultimate goal of serving the needs of the market and exploiting the full potential of the cultural and creative sectors as yet another tool to enhance competitiveness. the report promotes culture as an instrument of the european external action service's external policy and calls for the cooperation of universities with business to respond fully to the needs of large enterprises and the labour market. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area.

in writing. ‒ the as underlined in the riga declaration, gender equality is an area that would greatly benefit from enhanced cooperation between the eu and the eastern partnership countries. most of the challenges are common to the eu and its eastern neighbours. the report aims to contribute to giving women's rights and gender equality a higher political priority in the eastern partnership countries. there is a need for immediate action by the eastern partnership countries to increase equality between women and men in society, including the adoption of national action plans and cooperation with international organisations and civil society actors. i voted in favour of the report, which calls on the eastern partnership countries to address the shortcomings in their national anti-discrimination frameworks and to make greater use of anti-discrimination legislation in order to increase the degree of enforcement of the laws and to stop the violation of women's rights in these countries. it also stresses the need for awareness-raising campaigns and institutional changes to eliminate negative stereotypes about women that adversely affect all aspects of their participation in society.
in writing. ‒ the the report overly interferes in the internal affairs of other states with mandates for specific actions, e.g. promotion of mandatory quotas on electoral lists and the nordic model for the suppression of prostitution. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. equally serious is the fact that the report overlooks the fact that the strengthening of women's rights and, by extension, gender equality cannot be understood in the context of the neoliberal policies promoted by the eu in the eastern partnership countries. despite the report's individual positive references to ensuring women's participation in trade unions, combating discrimination at work, and calling for a strengthening of the network of quality care infrastructure for the elderly and dependent, these proposals cannot coexist and be strengthened over time because of the other measures being promoted by the eu, as is the case within the union. that's why we abstained.

in writing. ‒ the regulation (eu) 2015/848 of the european parliament and of the council of 20 may 2015 on insolvency proceedings entered into force on 26 june 2015. in december 2015, poland informed the commission of a substantial reform of its internal law on restructuring, which entered into force on 1 january 2016, and requested that the lists in annexes a and b to the regulation be amended accordingly. the commission has examined whether the request by poland complied with the requirements of the regulation and has submitted a proposal to the european parliament and the council to ensure that the scope of the regulation will be adapted to the current legal framework of the polish insolvency law from the moment of its application. in order to ensure the efficient management of cross-border insolvency proceedings of debtors whose centre of main interests is in a member state, the scope of the recast regulation should reflect the actual situation of national insolvency laws. this proposal aims to ensure that the scope of the recast regulation is adapted to the legal framework of the member states on insolvency in force at the time of application of the regulation.
in writing. ‒ the regulation (eu) 2015/848 of the european parliament and of the council on insolvency proceedings entered into force on 26 june 2015 and will apply from 26 june 2017. that regulation contains in its annexes lists of insolvency proceedings and insolvency practitioners concerned with its application. in december 2015, poland notified to the commission a substantial reform of its national legislation on restructuring and requested the amendment of the lists contained in annexes a and b to the regulation. on the basis of this request, the commission has submitted the proposal to the european parliament and the council, so the matter is purely technical since it does not contain any substantial amendment to the substance of the regulation, and that is why we voted in favour of the report.

in writing. ‒ we support the agreement, which supports historical fishing rights in this area and will be beneficial for the implementation of the revised cfp in the kattegat and skagerrak area.
in writing. ‒ the traditionally, fishermen do not fish in certain waters: fish for specific stocks or species and fish in waters where those stocks or species occur and also follow migratory species. this tradition predates the creation of states and the recognition of territorial waters and - much later - exclusive economic zones. the kattegat and skagerrak are a good example. since at least the middle ages, swedish, danish and norwegian fishermen have been fishing together in these waters for the same stocks. the fact that two of these countries have become member states has not affected the principle that fishermen from the three countries have the historical right to fish in the skagerrak and kattegat. respect for this historic right is of the utmost importance. the eu's common fisheries policy provides for a system of management of fish stocks in the waters covered by it and in its own way supports the historical rights of fishermen. the agreement also supports historical fishing rights in this area and will be beneficial for the implementation of the revised cfp in the kattegat and skagerrak. we therefore voted in favour of the report so that the agreement can be adopted.
