in writing. ‒ the fishermen from denmark, norway and sweden have traditionally always fished together in the kattegat and skagerrak. this agreement supports the historical fishing rights of fishermen from denmark, norway and sweden in the kattegat and skagerrak, without prejudice to the rights of fishermen from other states, while also ensuring that appropriate fisheries management and conservation measures are taken in these areas. the agreement also supports the implementation of the reformed eu fisheries management system, established in line with the objectives and basic principles of the new common fisheries policy (cfp), in particular the introduction of the landing obligation and mandatory measures to maintain stocks above levels allowing maximum sustainable yield. i therefore voted in favour of the report.

in writing. ‒ the in the last decade, three legislative 'railway packages' have been designed to open up national markets and make railways more competitive and more interoperable at eu level. in order to complete the opening of the domestic market for rail passenger transport to competition, the european commission has proposed to revise the current regulation 1370/2007 by deleting the possibility of directly awarding public service contracts. in its proposal, the european commission addressed some market challenges for new entrants that prevent them from competing with the incumbent (e.g. sncf, sncb, deutsche bahn), such as the lack of access to information needed to facilitate successful participation in public procurement procedures or the unavailability of a market for rolling stock. a second barrier to entry for new entrants is access to road equipment. i voted in favour of this report, which stresses the importance of improving the quality of passenger rail transport and enhancing its operational efficiency, thereby improving the competitiveness and attractiveness of the rail transport sector in relation to other modes of transport and further developing the single european railway area.

in writing. ‒ the this legislative proposal, also known as the 'governance directive', aims to open up the domestic rail passenger market to increase competitive pressure on domestic rail markets, to increase the quantity and improve the quality of passenger transport and to improve the governance of the infrastructure manager to ensure equal access to infrastructure. increased competition is expected to enhance the attractiveness of railways and make the railway sector more sensitive to customer needs, allowing railway undertakings to compete with other modes of transport. further development of high-speed passenger transport should also make railways more competitive by increasing their market share and contributing to climate change objectives. the increase in railway activity is also expected to increase the demand for skilled workers and rail service facility operators, as well as the demand for rolling stock, thus leading to the creation of new jobs in the railway construction industry. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of this report.

in writing. ‒ the report is about human rights and democracy in the world and eu policy in this area. the report contains a great deal of information. we stress that we are in no way opposed to human rights, but we are their first ambassadors. the report does, however, in fact further promote all the mechanisms currently in operation, many of which we have criticised at times. it supports the cfsp, the european army, the sophia mechanism, the eu's migration policy, etc., policies which we consider to be contrary to the objective of defending human rights. for all these reasons we abstained from this report.
in writing. ‒ the in an environment marked by instability and conflict in the middle east and parts of asia and africa, the ensuing migration crisis and repeated terrorist attacks against civilians, 2015 was a year of particular challenges for the european union and the international community in general. the islamic state has expanded its operations into areas of syria and iraq, committing mass atrocities and human rights violations, in particular against minority groups. a worrying trend of intimidation and attacks against activists and members of civil society was also observed. there is strong concern about the numerous, ever-increasing attempts to reduce the space for action of civil society and human rights defenders, and the increase in the number of repressive laws being enacted around the world, under the pretext of fighting terrorism (through the introduction of anti-terrorism laws). i voted in favour of the report because today's complex crises and widespread violations and abuses of human rights and fundamental freedoms require ever more determined efforts by the eu. the eu must respond to challenges through targeted action, with systematic and coordinated use of the instruments at its disposal and improved impact of its policies and tools on the ground.
in writing. ‒ we are committed to respecting and promoting human rights in all ways. the report, however, promotes all the mechanisms that the eu has which work in the opposite direction. it supports the cfsp, promotes the strengthening of the european army, the sophia mechanisms, the further militarization of the union and the strengthening of its interventionist action in the internal affairs of other states under the pretext of protecting democracy and human rights. for these reasons we abstained.

in writing. ‒ the the report concerns the implementation of the common foreign and security policy in the union. the report is an annual one and unfortunately, like the previous ones, it is part of the general philosophy of the need for the eu to become a strong international player. the need to align the foreign policies of the member states and to express themselves through a single voice is emphasized in several places. we understand that this serves the interests of the eu's ruling circles, which are aiming at full identification with the usa and nato. the report itself reaffirms our position that today there is a further militarization of foreign policy. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are fundamentally opposed to the militarisation of foreign policy that is being promoted and we are calling, among other things, for no funding of military missions from the eu budget and for a clear separation of the eu from nato. we call for the eu to become a pillar of peace and not war. that is why we voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ the the report, as every year, is part of the general philosophy of the need for the eu to become a strong international player. in addition to the alignment of member states' foreign policies and the expression of a single voice, the report highlights the commission's new plans for a common defence and military policy. this of course serves the eu's ruling circles who aim at full identification with the usa and nato. we voted against the report and our group put forward a minority view, opposing the militarization of foreign policy being promoted and calling for the immediate withdrawal of funding for military missions from the eu budget. the european union must be able to take action to combat terrorism and to prevent it from spreading.
in writing. ‒ the we support the report and stress that agricultural activity depends on biological processes. it is therefore highly dependent on climatic conditions and affected by plant and animal diseases natural factors beyond the control of farmers. the variation in farm performance is therefore much more uncertain than that of other sectors of the economy. income uncertainty mainly affects investment and innovation negatively and is capable of undermining the economic viability of holdings. in addition, fluctuations in production mean that agricultural markets have an inherent tendency to be unstable. it is widely accepted that excessive volatility benefits neither producers (by affecting market signals and making incomes uncertain) nor consumers (by affecting their food choice).
in writing. ‒ the agricultural activity depends on biological processes. it is therefore highly dependent on climatic conditions and affected by plant and animal diseases and natural factors beyond the control of farmers. the variation in farm performance is therefore much more uncertain than that of other sectors of the economy. income uncertainty mainly affects investment and innovation negatively and is capable of undermining the economic viability of holdings. in addition, fluctuations in production mean that agricultural markets have an inherent tendency to be unstable. it is generally accepted that excessive volatility benefits neither producers (by affecting market signals and making incomes uncertain) nor consumers (by affecting their food choice). i therefore voted in favour of the above resolution.
in writing. ‒ the the instability and volatility of prices have always characterised the agricultural market and in recent years, due to the rapid succession of supply, demand and price disturbances, they have become a structural feature of agriculture, both at european and global level. unfortunately, the current cap has failed to deal with the high volatility of prices on agricultural markets and places an excessive burden on the producer. the report, which i voted in favour of, after analysing the current situation and identifying the main problems, proposes various improvements for the forthcoming cap reform, such as strengthened sectoral organisation and syndication, better protection of farmers' incomes by creating risk management tools adapted to price volatility, greater market transparency by creating price observatories.

in writing. ‒ we believe it is essential to recognise the demands of thalidomide survivors in europe to ensure their autonomy, access to healthcare and a decent quality of life.
in writing. ‒ the thalidomide was used as a medicine to combat morning sickness in pregnant women, as well as to treat headaches, insomnia and colds in the late 1950s and early 1960s. this medicine has been on the market in several european countries for several years and has been shown to cause serious side effects in pregnant women whose babies are born with birth defects. this tragedy has affected several member states, and there the victims continue to seek a solution to cover the costs associated with the disease and their physical condition, which is deteriorating over time. this shows the importance of effective eu legislation to ensure that medicinal products placed on the market are subject to high standards of quality and safety. i have therefore voted in favour of the motion for a resolution.
in writing. ‒ the thalidomide was used as a morning sickness medication in pregnant women, as well as for the treatment of headaches, insomnia and colds, in the late 1950s and early 1960s. this treatment proved to have very serious side effects for pregnant women, whose babies were born with deformities. the resolution in question relates to financial support for the victims of thalidomide, which is why we voted in favour.
